消除乙型和丙型肝炎

Hepatitis B and C Elimination
作者： Nahum Méndez-Sánchez, Mariana M Ramírez-Mejía, Guadalupe Ponciano-Rodríguez
摘要中文翻译2016年,世界卫生组织设定了到2030年消除丙型肝炎病毒(HCV)这一公共卫生威胁的目标;其中包括新感染人数减少 90%、HCV 相关 死亡率 减少 65%、90% 感染者的 诊断 以及 80% 符合资格者的 治疗 减少。尽管直接作用的 抗病毒药物 (DAA) 在技术上可以实现病毒治疗,但拉丁美洲的进展仍然参差不齐。在这篇结构化叙述性审查和国家级政策规划中,我们评估了选定拉丁美洲国家目前的 HCV 流行病学、筛查和诊断途径、护理模式、DAA 获取、国家指南和 监测 能力。虽然正式消除承诺变得越来越普遍,但普遍或扩大筛查、反射诊断途径、分散护理和综合纵向登记的实施情况仍然不平衡。持续存在的缺陷与注射吸毒者(PWID)、被监禁人群、移民和其他难以获得常规护理的群体尤其相关。国际经验支持将现场护理和反射测试、监狱测试和 治疗、减少伤害整合和权力下放作为缩小这些差距的策略。在实现持续的病毒学反应后,消除计划还必须解决残留纤维化、肝细胞癌风险、代谢性和酒精相关的肝损伤,以及仍然暴露于病毒的人群中丙型肝炎病毒再感染的风险。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查的重点是消除 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染,检查其 流行病学、筛查和诊断途径、护理模式、获得直接作用的 抗病毒药物 (DAA)、国家指南以及 监测 在选定拉丁美洲国家的能力。核心问题是当前的策略是否能够实现世界卫生组织到 2030 年减少 HBV 相关感染和 死亡率 的目标。
证据基础与方法
该审查是结构化叙述性审查和国家级政策规划。它不报告特定的数据库、搜索日期或资格标准。这些方法涉及评估乙肝消除计划的现状,包括其在不同人群和地区的实施情况和有效性。
共识与结论
虽然国际经验支持诸如定点护理和反射测试、监狱测试和 治疗、减少伤害一体化和权力下放等策略,但拉丁美洲的进展仍然参差不齐。这些策略被认为是缩小乙肝消除差距的关键。
争议与缺口
乙肝消除计划持续存在的缺陷与弱势群体尤其相关,例如注射吸毒者(PWID)、被监禁者、移民和其他难以获得常规护理的人。证据凸显了普遍或扩大筛查、反射诊断途径、分散护理和综合纵向登记的实施不平衡。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要有针对性的研究和实践改进,包括更好的 监测、扩大 DAA 的获取范围以及针对高风险群体的定制干预措施。它还表明整合减少危害策略和改善诊断和护理途径以实现乙肝消除目标的重要性。

Hepatitis B and C Elimination

Authors: Nahum Méndez-Sánchez, Mariana M Ramírez-Mejía, Guadalupe Ponciano-Rodríguez
Original abstract (English)
In 2016, the World Health Organization set goals to eliminate the hepatitis C virus (HCV) as a public health threat by 2030; these include a 90% reduction in new infections, a 65% reduction in HCV-related mortality, the diagnosis of 90% of infected individuals, and the treatment of 80% of those eligible. Although direct-acting antivirals (DAAs) make a viral cure technically achievable, progress in Latin America remains heterogeneous. In this structured narrative review and country-level policy mapping, we assess the current epidemiology of HCV, screening and diagnostic pathways, care models, access to DAAs, national guidelines, and surveillance capabilities in select Latin American countries. While formal elimination commitments are becoming more common, universal or expanded screening, reflex diagnostic pathways, decentralized care, and integrated longitudinal registries remain unevenly implemented. Persistent deficiencies are particularly relevant for people who inject drugs (PWID), incarcerated populations, migrants, and other groups with barriers to conventional care. International experience supports point-of-care and reflex testing, prison-based testing and treatment, harm reduction integration, and decentralization as strategies to close these gaps. After achieving a sustained virological response, elimination programs must also address residual fibrosis, hepatocellular carcinoma risk, metabolic and alcohol-related liver injury, and the risk of HCV reinfection in populations that remain exposed to the virus.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the elimination of hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, examining its epidemiology, screening and diagnostic pathways, care models, access to direct-acting antivirals (DAAs), national guidelines, and surveillance capabilities in select Latin American countries. The central question is whether current strategies can achieve the World Health Organization's goals for reducing HBV-related infections and mortality by 2030.
Evidence base and method
The review is a structured narrative review and country-level policy mapping. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria. The methods involve assessing the current state of HBV elimination programs, including their implementation and effectiveness in different populations and regions.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that while international experience supports strategies such as point-of-care and reflex testing, prison-based testing and treatment, harm reduction integration, and decentralization, progress in Latin America remains heterogeneous. These strategies are considered key to closing gaps in HBV elimination.
Controversies and gaps
Persistent deficiencies in HBV elimination programs are particularly relevant for vulnerable populations such as people who inject drugs (PWID), incarcerated individuals, migrants, and others with barriers to conventional care. The evidence highlights uneven implementation of universal or expanded screening, reflex diagnostic pathways, decentralized care, and integrated longitudinal registries.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for targeted research and practice improvements, including better surveillance, expanded access to DAAs, and tailored interventions for high-risk groups. It also suggests the importance of integrating harm reduction strategies and improving diagnostic and care pathways to achieve HBV elimination goals.

针对乙型肝炎病毒表面抗原清除的靶向策略:从机制探索到临床转化的进展综述

Hepatitis B surface antigen-targeting strategies for clearance: A review of progress from mechanistic exploration to clinical translation
作者： Li Wang
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 的持续存在阻碍了 慢性乙型肝炎 患者实现 功能性治愈。抑制或消除 HBsAg 并打破 宿主 免疫耐受同时恢复 免疫应答 的策略成为免疫治疗导向的临床治愈的关键组成部分。靶向 HBsAg 的药物包括单克隆抗体、小干扰 RNA、反义寡核苷酸、基因编辑技术、衣壳组装调节剂和针对分泌途径的治疗药物。免疫检查点抑制剂和其他基于免疫的清除方法也包含在该战略框架内。这些试剂针对 HBsAg 生命周期及其源模板,包括共价闭合环状 DNA 和 宿主 基因组 中的集成 乙型肝炎病毒 DNA。本文通过综合临床前研究和临床试验的最新信息对这些策略进行综述,总结其机制和功效,讨论组合的挑战和前景,旨在为慢性乙型肝炎治疗研究提供科学依据。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是针对 慢性乙型肝炎 患者清除乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 的策略。核心问题是如何抑制或消除HBsAg并打破宿主免疫耐受以恢复免疫应答,这对于实现功能性治愈至关重要。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了临床前研究和临床试验的最新信息。它包括一系列针对 HBsAg 的药物,例如单克隆抗体、小干扰 RNA、反义寡核苷酸、基因编辑技术、衣壳组装调节剂和针对分泌途径的治疗药物。该审查还将免疫检查点抑制剂和其他基于免疫的清除方法纳入战略框架内。
共识与结论
该综述强调,针对 HBsAg 及其源模板的药物,包括共价闭合环状 DNA 和 宿主 基因组 中的集成 乙型肝炎病毒 DNA,对于免疫治疗导向的临床治愈至关重要。这些策略旨在解决 HBsAg 的持续存在问题,这是 慢性乙型肝炎 患者实现 功能性治愈 的主要障碍。
争议与缺口
该审查确定了这些策略的开发和应用中的挑战,包括需要进行组合以提高疗效。目前缺乏关于这些干预措施的长期结果及其对不同 HBV 基因型的有效性的全面数据。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,未来的研究应侧重于优化 HBsAg 靶向药物和免疫疗法的组合。它还强调了进一步研究的重要性,以评估这些策略在各种 HBV 基因型中的有效性,并解决当前方法的局限性。

Hepatitis B surface antigen-targeting strategies for clearance: A review of progress from mechanistic exploration to clinical translation

Authors: Li Wang
Original abstract (English)
Hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) persistence hinders the achievement of a functional cure in patients with chronic hepatitis B. Strategies to suppress or eliminate HBsAg and break the host immune tolerance while restoring immune response become key components of immune therapy-directed clinical cure. Agents that target HBsAg include monoclonal antibodies, small interfering RNAs, antisense oligonucleotides, gene editing technologies, capsid assembly modulators, and therapeutic agents targeting secretion pathways. Immune checkpoint inhibitors and other immune-based clearance approaches are also included within this strategic framework. These agents target the HBsAg lifecycle and its source templates, including covalently closed circular DNA and integrated hepatitis B virus DNA in the host genome. This article reviews these strategies by synthesizing the latest information from preclinical studies and clinical trials, summarises their mechanisms and efficacy, discusses challenges and prospects for combinations, and aims to provide a scientific basis for chronic hepatitis B curative research.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on strategies targeting the Hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) for clearance in patients with chronic hepatitis B. The central question is how to suppress or eliminate HBsAg and break host immune tolerance to restore immune response, which is essential for achieving a functional cure.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes the latest information from preclinical studies and clinical trials. It includes a range of agents targeting HBsAg, such as monoclonal antibodies, small interfering RNAs, antisense oligonucleotides, gene editing technologies, capsid assembly modulators, and therapeutic agents targeting secretion pathways. The review also incorporates immune checkpoint inhibitors and other immune-based clearance approaches within the strategic framework.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that agents targeting HBsAg and its source templates, including covalently closed circular DNA and integrated hepatitis B virus DNA in the host genome, are critical for immune therapy-directed clinical cure. These strategies aim to address the persistence of HBsAg, which is a major barrier to achieving a functional cure in chronic hepatitis B patients.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies challenges in the development and application of these strategies, including the need for combinations to enhance efficacy. There is a lack of comprehensive data on the long-term outcomes of these interventions and their effectiveness across different HBV genotypes.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that future research should focus on optimizing combinations of HBsAg-targeting agents and immune-based therapies. It also emphasizes the importance of further studies to evaluate the efficacy of these strategies across various HBV genotypes and to address the limitations in current methodologies.

免疫功能低下人群的 乙型肝炎病毒 重新激活:混合伞式综述和从头荟萃分析——血清学状态和免疫抑制暴露的证据图

Hepatitis B virus reactivation across immunocompromised populations: a hybrid umbrella review and de novo meta-analysis — an evidence map by serological status and immunosuppressive exposure
作者： Mengxia Yan, Ying Chen, Kaijie Yao, Yun Bao, Wen Li, Han Zhong, Lei Chen, Bin Wu
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 乙型肝炎病毒 再激活是免疫抑制治疗的一种可预防但管理不一致的并发症。尽管广泛建议对 HBsAg+ 个体进行 抗病毒药物 预防,但在解决 HBV 感染、新兴免疫疗法、生物制剂、移植和区域环境方面证据的确定性和适用性仍不清楚。目的 我们旨在综合免疫功能低下人群中 HBVr 的荟萃分析证据,并根据 HBV 血清学状态和免疫抑制暴露绘制证据的确定性。方法本研究采用了两级分析框架。第一级涉及综述级综合:对2014年1月至2025年3月期间在六个数据库中发表的系统综述和荟萃分析进行系统检索;使用 AMSTAR-2 工具评估方法学质量;评论之间的重叠程度使用修正覆盖面积进行量化。第二级涉及对初级研究进行从头荟萃分析:从搜索结果中提取来自无重复初级研究的数据,并使用随机效应模型来计算汇总的 发病率 估计值。针对两个水平的结果创建了证据图,并按血清学状态和免疫抑制暴露进行分层。结果本研究共纳入10项系统评价/荟萃分析,涵盖重复数据删除后的275项原始研究。在综述层面,纳入综述的方法学质量较低或非常低。综合叙述表明,HBsAg+ 个体中 HBVr 的风险最高,其次是 HBsAg-/抗 HBc+ 个体。初步研究的从头荟萃分析进一步量化了这些风险:HBsAg+ 个体中 HBVr 的汇总 发病率 为 11.44%(95% CI:9.17%–13.86%),而 HBsAg-/抗 HBc+ 个体中的 发病率 为 0.99%(95% CI:0.41%–1.74%);抗 HBs- 亚组的估计值高于抗 HBs+ 亚组的估计值。然而,所有亚组分析均观察到显着的异质性;因此,这些总体估计值应被视为源自高度异质性证据基础的广泛总结,而不是可直接外推至临床实践的精确 发病率 率。预防性 发病率 治疗与 HBsAg+ 个体中 HBVr 较低的 发病率 相关;然而,对于乙肝病毒感染已治愈的患者的获益证据以及比较不同 抗病毒药物 药物的头对头证据仍然不足。结论 证据支持 HBsAg+ 患者基于风险的预防,但解决 HBV 感染、生物/ICI 治疗和移植仍存在不确定性。该证据图可以为指南更新和未来的研究提供信息。

Hepatitis B virus reactivation across immunocompromised populations: a hybrid umbrella review and de novo meta-analysis — an evidence map by serological status and immunosuppressive exposure

Authors: Mengxia Yan, Ying Chen, Kaijie Yao, Yun Bao, Wen Li, Han Zhong, Lei Chen, Bin Wu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Hepatitis B virus reactivation is a preventable but inconsistently managed complication of immunosuppressive therapy. Although antiviral prophylaxis is widely recommended for HBsAg+ individuals, the certainty and applicability of evidence across resolved HBV infection, emerging immunotherapies, biologics, transplantation, and regional settings remain unclear. Aim We aimed to synthesize meta-analytic evidence on HBVr in immunocompromised populations and to map the certainty of evidence according to HBV serological status and immunosuppressive exposure. Methods This study employed a two-level analytical framework. The first level involved review-level synthesis: a systematic search was conducted for systematic reviews and meta-analyses published in six databases between January 2014 and March 2025; methodological quality was assessed using the AMSTAR‐2 tool; and the degree of overlap among reviews was quantified using the Corrected Covered Area. The second level involved a de novo meta-analysis of primary studies: data from duplicate-free primary studies were extracted from the search results, and a random-effects model was used to calculate pooled incidence estimates. Evidence maps were created for the results of both levels, stratified by serological status and immunosuppressive exposure. Results This study included a total of 10 systematic reviews/meta-analyses, covering 275 original studies after deduplication. At the review level, the methodological quality of the included reviews was low or very low. A narrative synthesis showed that the risk of HBVr was highest among HBsAg+ individuals, followed by HBsAg-/anti-HBc+ individuals. The de novo meta-analysis of primary studies further quantified these risks: the pooled incidence of HBVr in HBsAg+ individuals was 11.44% (95% CI:​9.17%–13.86%), whilst that in HBsAg-/anti-HBc+ individuals was 0.99% (95% CI:​0.41%–1.74%); estimates for the anti-HBs- subgroup were higher than those for the anti-HBs+ subgroup. However, significant heterogeneity was observed across all subgroup analyses; these overall estimates should therefore be regarded as broad summaries derived from a highly heterogeneous evidence base, rather than precise incidence rates directly extrapolatable to clinical practice. Prophylactic antiviral therapy is associated with a lower incidence of HBVr in HBsAg+ individuals; however, evidence of benefit in patients with resolved HBV infection and head-to-head evidence comparing different antiviral drugs remains insufficient. Conclusion Evidence supports risk-based prophylaxis in HBsAg+ patients, but uncertainties persist for resolved HBV infection, biologic/ICI therapies, and transplantation. This evidence map may inform guideline updates and future research.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Aim We aimed to synthesize meta-analytic evidence on HBVr in immunocompromised populations and to map the certainty of evidence according to HBV serological status and immunosuppressive exposure.
Evidence base and method
Methods This study employed a two-level analytical framework.
Consensus and conclusions
Results This study included a total of 10 systematic reviews/meta-analyses, covering 275 original studies after deduplication.
Controversies and gaps
However, significant heterogeneity was observed across all subgroup analyses; these overall estimates should therefore be regarded as broad summaries derived from a highly heterogeneous evidence base, rather than precise incidence rates directly extrapolatable to clinical practice.
Research and practice implications
This evidence map may inform guideline updates and future research.

HBV/HIV-1 合并感染中的双重 功能性治愈:对当前证据和概念框架的批判性评价的叙述性回顾。

Dual Functional Cure in HBV/HIV-1 Co-infection: A Narrative Review for Critical Appraisal of Current Evidence and Conceptual Framework.
作者： Ye Zhang, Mei-Juan Peng, Yu-Shen Liu, Tian-Ping Wang, Jing Li, Yan Cheng, Yan-Yan Zhao, Wei-Lu Zhang, Xin Wei, Wen Kang
摘要中文翻译乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和人类免疫缺陷病毒-1 (HIV-1) 的综合症影响着全世界数百万人。每种病毒都会加剧另一种病毒的 发病机制,导致肝脏疾病加速并增加 死亡率。虽然联合抗逆转录病毒疗法 (cART) 可抑制这两种病毒,但它很少能达到 功能性治愈,功能性治愈 被定义为 HBV 的持续乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 血清清除和 HIV-1 的持久无 cART 缓解,因为持续存在病毒 储存宿主(HBV 的共价闭合环状 DNA 和 HIV-1 的整合原病毒 DNA)。最近 HBV 和 HIV-1 治愈研究的并行进展引起了人们对“双重 功能性治愈”的极大兴趣。在这篇综述中,我们批判性地评估了当前的证据是否支持这一概念。我们首先总结了 HBV/HIV-1 合并感染的 流行病学,然后概述了合并感染特定的障碍和机会,以区分双重治疗和两种单一疾病策略的简单叠加。接下来,我们从单一感染与合并感染的证据水平、安全问题和转化障碍方面比较主要治疗策略(基于干扰素、免疫检查点阻断、Toll 样受体激动剂、广泛中和抗体、基因编辑和潜伏期靶向)。最后,我们提出了一个连续的、个性化的框架作为假设生成工具,强调大多数步骤仍然是需要专门的双重感染试验的推断。最后,我们确定了可行的研究重点,包括患者选择标准、终点选择和肝炎发作的管理。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和人类免疫缺陷病毒-1 (HIV-1) 的综合症影响着全世界数百万人。我们严格评估当前证据是否支持 HBV/HIV-1 合并感染中的“双重 功能性治愈”概念。
证据基础与方法
这篇综述是对 HBV/HIV-1 合并感染现有证据的叙述性综合。它概述了混合感染的 流行病学,比较了单一感染与混合感染的治疗策略,并评估了安全问题和转化障碍。该综述未报告荟萃分析或汇总估计。
共识与结论
目前的证据表明,联合抗逆转录病毒疗法 (cART) 可抑制 HBV 和 HIV-1,但由于病毒 储存宿主 持续存在,很少能达到 功能性治愈。双重 功能性治愈 定义为 HBV 的持续 HBsAg 血清清除和 HIV-1 的持久无 cART 缓解,仍然是一个正在严格评估的概念。
争议与缺口
该审查强调,拟议的双 功能性治愈 连续、个性化框架中的大多数步骤仍然是需要专门的共感染试验的推断。缺乏关于人类双重感染的双重 功能性治愈 策略的直接证据。
科研与实践启示
可操作的研究重点包括定义患者选择标准、选择适当的终点以及在 治疗 期间管理肝炎发作。这些优先事项旨在指导 HBV/HIV-1 双重感染中双重 功能性治愈 的未来研究和临床方法。

Dual Functional Cure in HBV/HIV-1 Co-infection: A Narrative Review for Critical Appraisal of Current Evidence and Conceptual Framework.

Authors: Ye Zhang, Mei-Juan Peng, Yu-Shen Liu, Tian-Ping Wang, Jing Li, Yan Cheng, Yan-Yan Zhao, Wei-Lu Zhang, Xin Wei, Wen Kang
Original abstract (English)
The syndemic of hepatitis B virus (HBV) and human immunodeficiency virus-1 (HIV-1) affects millions of people worldwide. Each virus exacerbates the pathogenesis of the other, leading to accelerated liver disease and increased mortality. While combined antiretroviral therapy (cART) suppresses both viruses, it rarely achieves a functional cure, which is defined as sustained hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) seroclearance for HBV and durable cART-free remission for HIV-1, due to persistent viral reservoirs (covalently closed circular DNA for HBV and integrated proviral DNA for HIV-1). Recent parallel advances in HBV and HIV-1 cure research have generated considerable interest in a "dual functional cure". In this review, we critically evaluate whether current evidence supports such a concept. We first summarize the epidemiology of HBV/HIV-1 coinfection, and then outline coinfection‐specific barriers and opportunities that distinguish a dual cure from a simple superposition of two single‐disease strategies. Next, we compare major cure strategies (interferon‐based, immune checkpoint blockade, Toll‐like receptor agonists, broadly neutralizing antibodies, gene editing, and latency targeting) in terms of evidence level in monoinfection versus coinfection, safety concerns, and translational barriers. Finally, we propose a sequential, personalized framework as a hypothesis‐generating tool, emphasizing that most steps remain extrapolations requiring dedicated coinfection trials. We conclude by defining actionable research priorities, including patient selection criteria, end point choices, and management of hepatitis flares.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The syndemic of hepatitis B virus (HBV) and human immunodeficiency virus-1 (HIV-1) affects millions of people worldwide. We critically evaluate whether current evidence supports the concept of a "dual functional cure" in HBV/HIV-1 co-infection.
Evidence base and method
This review is a narrative synthesis of existing evidence on HBV/HIV-1 co-infection. It outlines the epidemiology of the co-infection, compares cure strategies in monoinfection versus coinfection, and evaluates safety concerns and translational barriers. The review does not report a meta-analysis or pooled estimate.
Consensus and conclusions
Current evidence suggests that combined antiretroviral therapy (cART) suppresses both HBV and HIV-1 but rarely achieves a functional cure due to persistent viral reservoirs. A dual functional cure, defined as sustained HBsAg seroclearance for HBV and durable cART-free remission for HIV-1, remains a concept under critical appraisal.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that most steps in the proposed sequential, personalized framework for dual functional cure remain extrapolations requiring dedicated coinfection trials. There is a lack of direct evidence on dual functional cure strategies in human co-infection.
Research and practice implications
Actionable research priorities include defining patient selection criteria, choosing appropriate endpoints, and managing hepatitis flares during treatment. These priorities aim to guide future studies and clinical approaches for dual functional cure in HBV/HIV-1 co-infection.

Patterns of Patient Visits to a Gastroenterology Clinic in a Tertiary Hospital in Sub-Saharan Africa - A Retrospective Study.

Patterns of Patient Visits to a Gastroenterology Clinic in a Tertiary Hospital in Sub-Saharan Africa - A Retrospective Study.
作者： S N Chukwurah, E S Obi, C E Omaliko, C M Nwaneki, E D Malachy, C F Udigwe, H C Nwadimkpa, A B Okafor, J C Ezigbo, M I Amobi

Patterns of Patient Visits to a Gastroenterology Clinic in a Tertiary Hospital in Sub-Saharan Africa - A Retrospective Study.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-11-26  |  Publisher: Bioengineer.org
B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

乙型肝炎和 HIV 双重感染 功能性治愈 需要专门的共感染临床试验 - Bioengineer.org

Dual Hepatitis B and HIV Functional Cure Requires Dedicated Coinfected Clinical Trials - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A recent review published in Infectious Diseases and Therapy explores the challenges of achieving a dual functional cure for patients coinfected with hepatitis B virus (HBV) and HIV-1. The study highlights that co-infection is a global syndemic affecting around 3.4 million people, with regional variations in prevalence. It notes that combination antiretroviral therapy suppresses both viruses but rarely eliminates them. The review emphasizes the persistence of viral reservoirs, particularly covalently closed circular DNA for HBV and integrated proviral DNA for HIV-1, as a major obstacle. It also discusses the bidirectional interaction between the two viruses, which exacerbates immune dysfunction and accelerates liver disease progression. The findings suggest that curative strategies must target both reservoirs within the liver, as peripheral blood compartments are insufficient. Additionally, the review raises ethical concerns about end point assessment for HIV functional cure, which requires treatment interruption. The study underscores the need for dedicated clinical trials to address the unique challenges of dual HBV and HIV-1 infection.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 11 月 26 日发表。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但研究结果基于 2024 年的数据和正在进行的临床观察。
地点与对象
这些证据涉及全球人群,特别提到了西非和中非、亚太地区、拉丁美洲、西欧和北美的双重感染者。它还包括来自尼日利亚、加纳、埃塞俄比亚和中国的数据。
事件
《传染病与治疗》上发表的一篇综合叙述性评论批判性地评估了在同时感染 乙型肝炎病毒 和 HIV-1 的患者中实现双重 功能性治愈 的科学可行性。该综述强调了为合并感染者制定治疗策略的挑战。
规模、影响与风险
The evidence states that co-infection affects approximately 3.4 million people globally. Regional pooled prevalence ranges from 6.0 percent in Western Europe and North America to 12.4 percent in West and Central Africa. The review also notes that coinfected patients experience slower CD4 recovery and heightened oncological vulnerability, with elevated liver cancer incidence in North American and Swiss cohorts.
应对、状态与不确定性
该综述认为病毒 储存宿主 的持续存在是实现双重 功能性治愈 的主要障碍。它强调需要靶向肝组织内的 储存宿主,而不是集中于外周血区室。该研究还讨论了终点评估的伦理挑战,这需要进行分析性 治疗 中断来确认 HIV 功能性治愈。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dual Hepatitis B and HIV Functional Cure Requires Dedicated Coinfected Clinical Trials - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A recent review published in Infectious Diseases and Therapy explores the challenges of achieving a dual functional cure for patients coinfected with hepatitis B virus (HBV) and HIV-1. The study highlights that co-infection is a global syndemic affecting around 3.4 million people, with regional variations in prevalence. It notes that combination antiretroviral therapy suppresses both viruses but rarely eliminates them. The review emphasizes the persistence of viral reservoirs, particularly covalently closed circular DNA for HBV and integrated proviral DNA for HIV-1, as a major obstacle. It also discusses the bidirectional interaction between the two viruses, which exacerbates immune dysfunction and accelerates liver disease progression. The findings suggest that curative strategies must target both reservoirs within the liver, as peripheral blood compartments are insufficient. Additionally, the review raises ethical concerns about end point assessment for HIV functional cure, which requires treatment interruption. The study underscores the need for dedicated clinical trials to address the unique challenges of dual HBV and HIV-1 infection.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Dual Hepatitis B and HIV Functional Cure Requires Dedicated Coinfected Clinical Trials by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Dual Hepatitis B and HIV Functional Cure Requires Dedicated Coinfected Clinical Trials Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive narrative review published in Infectious Diseases and Therapy critically evaluates the scientific feasibility of achieving a dual functional cure in patients with concurrent hepatitis B virus and HIV-1 infections. Co-infection constitutes a severe global syndemic affecting approximately 3.4 million people worldwide, in which each pathogen exacerbates the clinical course and morbidity of the other. Although combination antiretroviral therapy suppresses viral replication for both pathogens, it requires lifelong adherence and rarely eliminates either infection. A functional cure is defined in hepatitis B as sustained seroclearance of hepatitis B surface antigen for at least twenty-four weeks alongside undetectable viral DNA, and in HIV-1 as durable treatment-free virological control. The primary obstacle remains the persistence of cellular viral reservoirs, specifically covalently closed circular DNA for hepatitis B and integrated proviral DNA for HIV-1. The global distribution of dual infection is highly uneven, shaped by shared transmission routes including sexual contact, parenteral exposure, and perinatal transmission. At the close of 2024, approximately 40.8 million individuals were living with HIV-1 globally, among whom pooled prevalence of hepatitis B surface antigen positivity was 8.4 percent, representing roughly 3.4 million coinfected individuals. Regional pooled prevalence reaches 12.4 percent in West and Central Africa and 9.8 percent across the Asia-Pacific region, compared to 5.1 percent in Latin America and 6.0 percent in Western Europe and North America. Independent cohort studies in Nigeria, Ghana, and Ethiopia documented coinfection rates between 10 and 15 percent, while China national treatment registry data identified coinfection in 8.7 percent of 33,861 patients. Key documented risk factors include male sex, detention, higher HIV load, lower CD4 cell counts, and lack of hepatitis B vaccination. The biological interaction between the two viruses is bidirectional and deleterious rather than a simple co-occurrence. HIV-1 infection causes severe CD4 T-cell depletion and chronic immune activation, upregulating programmed death-1 and programmed death ligand-1 pathways that induce profound T-cell exhaustion. This immunosuppressive environment impairs essential hepatitis B immune clearance mechanisms normally mediated by cytotoxic CD8 T cells, natural killer cells, neutralizing antibodies, and interferon signaling pathways. Conversely, hepatitis B infection alters the clinical trajectory of HIV-1. Coinfected patients present with lower baseline CD4 counts and elevated transaminases upon starting antiretroviral therapy, and experience slower early CD4 recovery over the initial four years. Furthermore, molecular studies demonstrate that the hepatitis B virus X protein directly enhances HIV-1 long terminal repeat transcription by interacting with host CCAAT/enhancer binding proteins and regulatory elements, confirming that hepatitis B actively modulates HIV-1 persistence. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Anatomically, the human liver serves as a shared sanctuary harboring viral reservoirs for both pathogens. Hepatitis B virus establishes stable intranuclear covalently closed circular DNA within hepatocytes, while HIV-1 infects hepatocytes, resident Kupffer cells, and liver-infiltrating CD4 T cells, persisting even throughout suppressive antiretroviral therapy. Microbial translocation across compromised intestinal mucosa releases lipopolysaccharide and soluble CD14 into circulation, driving intrahepatic expression of inflammatory chemokines like CXCL10 and CCL2. Elevated intrahepatic CXCL10 strongly correlates with accelerated liver fibrosis in coinfected cohorts. Concurrently, HIV-1 envelope glycoprotein gp120 binds chemokine receptor CCR5 on hepatic stellate cells to promote profibrogenic signaling, while enhanced hypoxia-inducible factor-1 alpha and transforming growth factor-beta-1 activity exacerbates collagen accumulation. Because hepatic HIV persistence maintains localized immune activation, curative regimens must target both viral reservoirs within hepatic tissue rather than focusing exclusively on peripheral blood compartments. This severe pathogenic interplay translates into accelerated liver disease progression and heightened oncological vulnerability. Clinical data from a Spanish cohort showed that 20 to 25 percent of men living with HIV-1 developed chronic hepatitis B following acute infection, compared to only 5 percent among immunocompetent adults. Hepatocellular carcinoma incidence reaches 1.8 cases per 1,000 person-years in North American coinfection cohorts and 1.7 cases in Swiss studies. Major independent risk factors for liver cancer include age over forty years, hepatitis C coinfection, alcohol use, HIV viremia exceeding 500 copies per milliliter, hepatitis B viral loads above 200 international units per milliliter, low CD4 counts, and pre-existing cirrhosis. Although tenofovir-containing regimens significantly reduce hepatocellular carcinoma incidence, increasing patient longevity elevates the lifetime risk of cirrhosis. Furthermore, a meta-analysis encompassing 12,382 participants revealed that hepatitis B coinfection significantly increases overall all-cause mortality among individuals receiving antiretroviral therapy. Discover more Study Biology Study Chemistry Read Health Guides In chronic hepatitis B monoinfection, curative benchmarks highlight the contrast between suppressive nucleoside analogs and immunomodulatory interventions. Approved oral agents like entecavir, tenofovir disoproxil fumarate, and tenofovir alafenamide clear circulating viral DNA in over 90 percent of patients within one year, yet achieve surface antigen seroclearance in only about 1 percent. By comparison, pegylated interferon-alpha strategies achieve surface antigen loss rates ranging from 3 to 65 percent depending on baseline criteria. In the OSST trial, switching from entecavir to pegylated interferon-alpha yielded 8.5 percent overall antigen seroclearance, rising to 22.2 percent in patients with antigen levels below 1,500 international units per milliliter and 77.8 percent below 200 international units. The NEW SWITCH trial recorded 14.4 percent and 20.7 percent seroclearance at 48 and 96 weeks, while the Everest study involving 15,896 patients achieved 26.4 percent loss. Novel agents such as bepirovirsen have also demonstrated meaningful functional cure outcomes. In HIV-1 research, curative strategies distinguish complete sterilizing cure from functional remission maintaining viremia below 50 copies per milliliter without antiretroviral therapy. Spontaneous control occurs in fewer than 1 percent of infected individuals, recognized as elite controllers who possess protective HLA class I alleles and effective immune responses. However, long-term remission in the Geneva patient transplanted with wild-type CCR5 cells and the second Berlin patient receiving heterozygous donor cells indicates that complete coreceptor absence is not strictly mandatory. Given the extreme risks of transplantation, scalable approaches focus on CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing to disrupt CCR5 or CXCR4 coreceptors, successfully conferring cellular resistance against viral entry in preclinical evaluations. Alternative functional cure pathways for HIV-1 involve latency-targeting interventions, broadly neutralizing antibodies, and immunomodulatory agents. Under the shock-and-kill strategy, latency-reversing agents including histone deacetylase inhibitors like vorinostat, romidepsin, and panobinostat are deployed to reactivate latent proviruses for immune clearance, while block-and-lock approaches utilize Tat inhibitors to silence transcription permanently. Concurrently, infusions of broadly neutralizing antibodies have demonstrated clinical efficacy; the TITAN trial established that 36 percent of participants sustained viral suppression beyond 25 weeks, while triple antibody combinations achieved control in 83 percent for at least 28 weeks. Pegylated interferon-alpha also possesses dual immunomodulatory potential, producing a median viral decline of 0.61 log10 copies per milliliter in HIV monoinfection and reducing CD4-associated viral reservoirs in coinfected patients with hepatitis C. Despite these individual advancements, their efficacy and translational feasibility in simultaneous hepatitis B and HIV coinfection remain essentially untested. Translating single-infection cure models into coinfection encounters both distinct immunological opportunities and severe clinical hazards. Coinfected individuals receiving long-term suppressive tenofovir-based antiretroviral therapy paradoxically achieve surface antigen clearance rates between 7 and 11 percent, exceeding 10 percent in select cohorts, well above the 1 percent annual rate seen in hepatitis B monoinfection. This phenomenon reflects immune reconstitution, where restoring CD4 counts disrupts pre-existing hepatitis B immune tolerance and provokes hepatic flares that frequently precede antigen loss. However, these hepatic flares pose severe risks of acute decompensation and fatal liver injury. Furthermore, end point assessment introduces a major ethical barrier: while hepatitis B functional cure can be confirmed on active therapy, establishing an HIV functional cure requires an analytical treatment interruption. In coinfected patients, interrupting antiretroviral therapy risks aggressive hepatitis B viral rebound, catastrophic hepatitis flares, and liver failure, and no established protocol currently exists for safe interruption. To navigate these challenges, the authors proposed a four-phase sequential framework as a hypothesis-generating model. Phase one establishes deep dual suppression through at least four to five years of continuous tenofovir-based therapy, which lowers hepatic inflammation, prevents fibrosis progression, and provides an immunological baseline without treatment interruption. Phase two applies retrospective criteria to select optimal candidates, prioritizing patients with surface antigen levels below 1,500 international units per milliliter, favorable genotypes like genotype B, CD4 counts above 350 cells per cubic millimeter, and previous immune flares. Phase three investigates combination immunotherapy by adding pegylated interferon-alpha for 24 to 48 weeks, potentially alongside checkpoint inhibitors, Toll-like receptor 8 agonists, or latency-reversing agents under strict safety monitoring. Phase four involves consolidation, flare monitoring, and hepatocellular carcinoma surveillance, restricting analytical treatment interruption exclusively to clinical trials with rigorous rescue protocols. Advancing this conceptual framework requires dedicated clinical trials and validated biomarkers specifically evaluated in coinfected populations. Early-phase trial designs such as AIDS Clinical Trials Group A5394 illustrate the meticulous safety protocols and biomarker-driven endpoints required for evaluating combination immunotherapies. In accordance with 2025 European Association for the Study of the Liver guidance, clinical studies must evaluate emerging hepatitis B biomarkers, including quantitative surface antigen, hepatitis B core-related antigen, and circulating viral RNA, in parallel with assays quantifying intact HIV reservoirs. Furthermore, single-cell multiomics technologies are needed to simultaneously track virus-specific T-cell responses within the same coinfected individuals, while genotype-guided strategies must address differences across viral lineages. Because historical clinical trials routinely excluded coinfected individuals due to toxicity concerns, the authors conclude that close collaboration between hepatology and HIV research communities is essential to replace theoretical extrapolations with robust clinical evidence. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Sensorimotor Axonal Polyneuropathy Revealing Chronic Hepatitis B Virus Infection in the Postpartum Period: A Case Report and Review of the Literature - Cureus

News Brief
This case report describes a 32-year-old woman who developed sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy with cerebellar syndrome and nystagmus during the postpartum period, leading to the diagnosis of previously unrecognized chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection. The patient delivered vaginally at home with preeclampsia and was readmitted three weeks post-delivery due to progressive lower limb impairment. Workup revealed positive hepatitis B surface antigen and total anti-HBc antibodies, along with an elevated HBV viral load, despite normal liver function tests. Antineutrophil cytoplasmic antibodies (ANCA) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) serology were negative, and vitamin B1 deficiency was identified as a potential confounding factor. Antiviral therapy with tenofovir was initiated, and follow-up was planned. The case underscores the importance of systematic screening for chronic HBV infection in patients with unexplained peripheral neuropathy, especially in the postpartum period, even in the absence of hepatic abnormalities. However, a comprehensive etiological workup is essential to rule out other differential diagnoses, including nutritional causes.
Original source text (English)
Abstract Extrahepatic manifestations of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection are well described; however, peripheral nervous system involvement is a rare and probably underdiagnosed manifestation. We report a case of sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy that led to the diagnosis of a previously unrecognized chronic HBV infection during the postpartum period. A 32-year-old woman, gravida 2 para 2, who delivered vaginally at home in the context of preeclampsia, developed partial functional impairment of both lower limbs during the postpartum period, accompanied by a static-kinetic cerebellar syndrome, bilateral horizontal nystagmus, and a peripheral neurogenic syndrome involving all four limbs, without digestive symptoms or clinical signs of liver disease. Workup revealed a positive hepatitis B surface antigen, positive total anti-HBc antibodies, negative HBe antigen, and an elevated HBV viral load, despite normal liver function tests. Antineutrophil cytoplasmic antibodies (ANCA) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) serology were negative. Complement levels, cryoglobulinemia testing, and nerve/muscle biopsy were not performed. Cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) analysis was normal, with no evidence of albuminocytologic dissociation. Vitamin B1 deficiency was identified, whereas vitamin B12 levels were normal and cold agglutinins were negative. Brain CT angiography and MRI were normal. Nerve conduction studies were consistent with sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy, predominantly affecting the lower limbs. Antiviral therapy with tenofovir was initiated, and clinical and paraclinical follow-up was planned. This presentation falls within the spectrum of extrahepatic neurological manifestations of HBV infection. The associated vitamin B1 deficiency represents an important confounding factor that must be considered in the diagnostic reasoning, together with the immunological particularities of the postpartum period. The treatment decision was guided by current international recommendations regarding early antiviral therapy for HBV infection in the presence of symptomatic extrahepatic manifestations. In patients presenting with unexplained peripheral neuropathy, particularly in a suggestive clinical context, screening for chronic HBV infection should be systematic, even in the absence of hepatic abnormalities. Nevertheless, a comprehensive etiological workup is essential to exclude relevant differential diagnoses before HBV infection is considered the sole underlying cause. Introduction Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection remains one of the leading causes of liver disease worldwide. According to the most recent WHO estimates, approximately 254 million people were living with chronic HBV infection in 2022, accounting for about 1.1 million deaths annually, mainly attributable to cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma. The 2026 global hepatitis report confirms the persistently high disease burden, with approximately 240 million chronic carriers reported in 2024, fewer than 5% of whom were receiving antiviral treatment [1,2]. Although liver involvement remains the most feared complication of this infection, approximately 20% of patients develop extrahepatic manifestations, including polyarteritis nodosa (PAN), non-rheumatoid arthritis, glomerulonephritis, non-Hodgkin lymphoma, and cryoglobulinemic vasculitis. These manifestations, which are independent of viral genotype, may precede, accompany, or occur in the absence of overt hepatic disease, significantly contributing to patient morbidity and impaired quality of life [3]. Peripheral nervous system involvement is among the least well-characterized extrahepatic manifestations of HBV infection. It may present as mononeuritis multiplex in the setting of necrotizing vasculitis, such as HBV-associated PAN, historically the neurological presentation most classically associated with HBV; as polyneuropathy associated with mixed cryoglobulinemia, which is considerably less common than in hepatitis C virus (HCV) infection; or, exceptionally, as Guillain-Barré syndrome (GBS) [3,4]. The postpartum period represents a distinct pathophysiological setting, characterized by rapid immune reconstitution following the relative immunological tolerance of pregnancy, which may unmask or exacerbate underlying infectious or dysimmune conditions [5]. We report the case of a patient who developed sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy associated with a cerebellar syndrome and nystagmus in the immediate postpartum period following a delivery complicated by preeclampsia, leading to the diagnosis of previously unrecognized chronic HBV infection. This case provides an opportunity to discuss, in light of a review of the literature, the spectrum of HBV-associated peripheral neurological manifestations, their differential diagnoses, particularly nutritional causes, in the specific context of the postpartum period, and the current diagnostic and therapeutic implications of these manifestations. Case Presentation History A 32-year-old woman, gravida 2 para 2 (with two living children, both delivered vaginally), with no significant past medical history apart from preeclampsia during the current pregnancy, delivered vaginally at home. She was hospitalized in the immediate postpartum period for completion of postpartum care and management of preeclampsia. Her initial clinical course was favorable, allowing her to be discharged. However, she was readmitted approximately three weeks after delivery due to rapidly progressive partial functional impairment of both lower limbs. There were no associated gastrointestinal symptoms, including abdominal pain, changes in bowel habits, gastrointestinal bleeding, or vomiting. Nevertheless, as discussed below, the absence of digestive symptoms does not exclude inadequate micronutrient intake or increased nutritional requirements during pregnancy and the peripartum period. Physical examination On admission, the patient was conscious and hemodynamically and respiratorily stable (blood pressure: 120/70 mmHg; heart rate: 113 beats/min; oxygen saturation: 100% on room air). Her conjunctivae were normally colored, with no jaundice or asterixis, findings that were specifically assessed given the hepatotropic viral infection subsequently suspected. Abdominal examination was unremarkable, with a soft, non-distended abdomen, no visible collateral venous circulation, no tenderness, guarding or rigidity, and no percussion dullness. There were no clinical stigmata of chronic liver disease or portal hypertension. Neurological examination revealed a static-kinetic cerebellar syndrome, bilateral horizontal nystagmus, and a peripheral neurogenic syndrome involving all four limbs. The coexistence of these three findings, namely cerebellar involvement, nystagmus, and polyneuropathy, is particularly relevant to the diagnostic discussion below, as this clinical constellation may also be suggestive of vitamin B1 deficiency. Laboratory, virological, and imaging findings The results of the etiological workup are summarized in Table 1. They revealed HBV infection, with a positive hepatitis B surface antigen, positive total anti-HBc antibodies, and negative HBe antigen, associated with a moderate viral load (8,590 IU/mL), despite normal liver function tests, with AST and ALT levels within normal limits. This serological profile, characterized by HBe antigen negativity, moderate viremia, and normal transaminase levels, is consistent with the so-called "indeterminate" or "gray zone" phase of chronic HBV infection, the management of which has recently been reassessed in international guidelines, as discussed. HIV, syphilis, and HCV serologies were negative, with the latter making HCV-associated cryoglobulinemia an unlikely alternative diagnosis. Testing for antineutrophil cytoplasmic antibodies (ANCA) was also negative, thereby reducing the likelihood of ANCA-associated vasculitis. Cold agglutinin testing was negative, making an immune-mediated hemolytic process or an underlying IgM monoclonal lymphoid disorder unlikely as an alternative explanation for the polyneuropathy. However, complement levels (C3 and C4), cryoglobulinemia testing, and nerve/muscle biopsy had not been performed at the time of manuscript preparation, representing an important limitation that is discussed below. Cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) analysis was normal, with no protein elevation or albuminocytologic dissociation, and both direct examination and culture were negative. Vitamin assessment revealed vitamin B1 deficiency, while vitamin B12 levels were normal. Creatine kinase levels were normal, arguing against an associated myopathic process. Brain CT angiography and MRI revealed no abnormalities that could explain the cerebellar syndrome or nystagmus. However, these normal imaging findings do not definitively exclude thiamine-deficiency-related brainstem or cerebellar involvement, as MRI findings may remain normal in some mild or incomplete forms of thiamine deficiency (Table 2). Nerve conduction studies The findings of the nerve conduction studies (NCS/EMG), performed approximately three weeks after delivery, are summarized in Table 3. These findings were consistent with a sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy, predominantly affecting the lower limbs. The preserved F-wave latencies and the absence of conduction block or temporal dispersion make an acute demyelinating process, such as an inflammatory polyradiculoneuropathy, unlikely, a point discussed in detail below. Diagnostic synthesis and initial management Taken together, the combination of sensorimotor axonal polyneuropathy affecting all four limbs, a static-kinetic cerebellar syndrome, and bilateral horizontal nystagmus, occurring during the postpartum period in a patient with previously undiagnosed chronic HBV infection, and with no alternative cause identified on brain imaging or CSF analysis, was considered suggestive of an HBV-related peripheral neurological manifestation. The negative ANCA results make ANCA-associated vasculitis unlikely, while negative HCV serology makes hepatitis C-related cryoglobulinemia an unlikely alternative cause. Similarly, the absence of cold agglutinins makes an underlying IgM monoclonal lymphoid disorder less likely. Collectively, these findings narrow the differential diagnosis and support a possible association with HBV infection. However, in the absence of complement measurements, cryoglobulinemia testing, and nerve/muscle biopsy, the presence of HBV-related vasculitis or cryoglobulinemia cannot be definitively established at this stage. The concomitant vitamin B1 deficiency should therefore be considered a potential contributing factor rather than the sole explanation for the neurological presentation. Treatment and follow-up Based on the available evidence and in accordance with current guidelines, antiviral therapy with tenofovir was initiated. Treatment had only recently been started at the time of manuscript preparation; therefore, no data on the clinical, virological, or electrophysiological response are yet available. Clinical, laboratory (viral load and transaminase levels), and electrophysiological follow-up are planned to assess the response to antiviral therapy and the progression of the polyneuropathy, as well as to guide any further escalation of management, including completion of the immunological workup or initiation of immunomodulatory therapy, if clinical improvement is not achieved. Discussion Epidemiology and pathophysiology of extrahepatic manifestations of HBV Chronic HBV infection is associated with extrahepatic manifestations in approximately 20% of patients, with no established correlation between these manifestations and viral genotype [3]. These manifestations are classically attributed to immunological mechanisms, particularly the deposition of circulating immune complexes formed by viral antigens, notably HBsAg, and their corresponding antibodies. This process triggers complement activation and inflammation of the vessel walls across vessels of various calibers. This mechanism is best documented in HBV-associated PAN, in which immune complex and complement deposits have been demonstrated in the walls of affected medium-sized vessels [4]. Historically, PAN was considered the archetypal HBV-associated vasculitis, with up to 30% to 50% of PAN cases attributed to HBV infection in studies published before the widespread introduction of HBV vaccination and systematic blood donor screening. The widespread implementation of HBV vaccination has since considerably reduced the incidence of this association in countries with high vaccine coverage, although it persists in regions with high viral endemicity [4]. Spectrum of HBV-related peripheral neurological involvement 1. Mononeuritis Multiplex and HBV-Associated Polyarteritis Nodosa HBV-associated PAN is a necrotizing vasculitis affecting medium-sized arteries and capable of involving multiple organs, including the skin, gastrointestinal tract, kidneys, and peripheral nervous system. Neurologically, it typically manifests as mononeuritis multiplex, often with an abrupt and asymmetric onset, initially predominantly sensory and subsequently progressing to motor involvement, resulting from ischemia of the vasa nervorum. In the largest published series to date, which included 115 patients with HBV-associated PAN, peripheral neurological involvement was among the most frequent and disabling manifestations of the disease, warranting a treatment approach combining antiviral therapy, a short course of corticosteroids, and plasma exchange [4]. 2. HBV-Related Cryoglobulinemic Polyneuropathy Mixed cryoglobulinemia, an immune complex-mediated small-vessel vasculitis, is predominantly associated with HCV, which accounts for approximately 90% of cases of cryoglobulinemic vasculitis. Its association with HBV is considerably less common, being reported in only 0.5% to 5.5% of mixed cryoglobulinemia cases in published series and affecting approximately 2% to 4% of patients with chronic hepatitis B [6]. When present, HBV-related cryoglobulinemia may nevertheless be complicated by sensory or sensorimotor, distal, length-dependent polyneuropathy, which is often painful and ascending. This occurs through a mechanism comparable to that described in HCV-related cryoglobulinemic neuropathy, involving both ischemia resulting from the deposition of cryoprecipitable immune complexes in the vasa nervorum and potentially direct antineuronal reactivity [6,7]. A recently published case of cryoglobulinemia associated with chronic HBV infection demonstrated marked clinical and laboratory improvement after a few months of antiviral treatment with tenofovir alafenamide, without the need for additional immunosuppressive therapy [5]. In our patient, negative HCV serology makes the most common cause of mixed cryoglobulinemia unlikely. However, cryoglobulinemia testing was not performed, meaning that this possibility remains uninvestigated rather than excluded. 3. Guillain-Barré Syndrome GBS has been reported in association with both acute and chronic HBV infection, particularly during episodes of viral reactivation, although this association remains rare and accounts for approximately 1% of all GBS cases [8,9]. Electrophysiologically, GBS is classically classified into a demyelinating form (acute inflammatory demyelinating polyradiculoneuropathy (AIDP)) and axonal forms (acute motor axonal neuropathy (AMAN) and acute motor and sensory axonal neuropathy (AMSAN)), based on well-established electrodiagnostic criteria that include conduction velocities, distal latencies, F-waves, and the assessment of conduction block or temporal dispersion [10,11]. The diagnosis of GBS is classically based on the combination of acute, progressive motor weakness, areflexia, and cerebrospinal fluid albuminocytologic dissociation, although the latter may be absent during the first few days of illness [12]. 4. Other Rare Presentations Other forms of peripheral nervous system involvement have been reported more anecdotally in HBV infection: plexopathies, radiculopathies, and isolated cranial nerve neuropathies. These presentations remain exceptional and rest essentially on isolated observations, which limits the strength of any pathophysiological conclusions that can be drawn from them [3]. Diagnostic approach and electrophysiological interpretation The evaluation of distal symmetric polyneuropathy classically follows a structured approach combining medical history, clinical examination, nerve conduction studies, and targeted laboratory investigations. American Academy of Neurology guidelines emphasize the importance of a first-line workup that includes at least blood glucose measurement, vitamin B12 levels, and serum protein electrophoresis, supplemented, as clinically indicated, by viral serologies and an immunological panel [13]. In our patient, nerve conduction studies enabled precise characterization of the neuropathy in terms of its nature (axonal rather than demyelinating) and distribution (sensorimotor, predominantly distal, and affecting the lower limbs). These findings subsequently guided the etiological workup toward acquired axonal neuropathies, including those associated with hepatotropic virus-related vasculitis, as well as nutritional, toxic, and metabolic causes. From a methodological standpoint, distinguishing axonal from demyelinating neuropathy relies on a combination of electrophysiological findings. Relatively preserved conduction velocities and distal latencies, together with a proportional reduction in response amplitudes, favor an axonal process. In contrast, disproportionate slowing of conduction velocities, prolonged distal latencies, conduction block or temporal dispersion, and prolonged or absent F waves favor a demyelinating process [10,11]. 一管检测可快速视觉检测 乙型肝炎病毒 RNA - Bioengineer.org

One-Tube Assay Delivers Rapid Visual Detection of Hepatitis B Virus RNA - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报研究小组开发了一种新型诊断平台 One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA,能够快速、超灵敏和特异性地视觉检测 乙型肝炎病毒 RNA。该平台将逆转录环介导的等温扩增与密封管内的激烈火球菌 Argonaute 介导的识别相结合。诊断工作流程在 90 分钟内完成,其中 45 分钟用于 RNA 制备,30 分钟用于扩增,10 分钟用于切割,不到两分钟用于使用基于金纳米粒子的侧流生物传感器进行视觉读数。该检测使用 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的血清样本进行了验证,与定量实时 PCR 达到 100% 的一致性。它为监测 HBV 感染和评估 治疗 反应提供了可靠的方法,且与其他微生物物种没有交叉反应。该研究强调了该平台支持即时诊断的潜力,特别是在资源有限的医疗保健环境中。

时间
该文章发表于2026年11月26日,描述的事件与乙型肝炎病毒 RNA检测诊断平台的开发有关,诊断工作流程在90分钟内完成。
地点与对象
该研究是在实验室环境中进行的,针对人类 乙型肝炎病毒 感染及其相关病症,包括 慢性乙型肝炎 和 HBV 相关肝细胞癌。
事件
研究人员开发了一种名为 One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA 的新型诊断平台,可提供快速、超灵敏和特异性的 乙型肝炎病毒 RNA 视觉检测。该平台使用 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的临床血清样本进行了验证,显示与定量实时 PCR 100% 一致。
规模、影响与风险
该测定证明每个反应的超灵敏检测限为 5 个拷贝,并且与其他微生物物种没有交叉反应。它可以帮助临床医生评估感染个体的治疗效果和 抗病毒药物 治疗 反应,所提供的证据中没有报告病例、死亡或暴露。
应对、状态与不确定性
该诊断平台经过系统评估和性能优化,分析验证证实了其可靠性。该研究是初步的,结果表明在资源有限的地区进行即时诊断的潜力。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

One-Tube Assay Delivers Rapid Visual Detection of Hepatitis B Virus RNA - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A research team has developed a novel diagnostic platform, One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA, which enables rapid, ultrasensitive, and specific visual detection of hepatitis B virus RNA. This platform combines reverse transcription loop-mediated isothermal amplification with Pyrococcus furiosus Argonaute-mediated identification within a sealed tube. The diagnostic workflow completes in ninety minutes, with forty-five minutes dedicated to RNA preparation, thirty minutes for amplification, ten minutes for cleavage, and less than two minutes for visual readout using a gold nanoparticle-based lateral flow biosensor. The assay was validated using serum samples from chronic hepatitis B patients, achieving 100% concordance with quantitative real-time PCR. It offers a reliable method for monitoring HBV infections and assessing treatment response, with no cross-reactivity against other microbial species. The study highlights the potential for this platform to support point-of-care diagnostics, particularly in resource-limited healthcare settings.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology One-Tube Assay Delivers Rapid Visual Detection of Hepatitis B Virus RNA by November 26, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 One-Tube Assay Delivers Rapid Visual Detection of Hepatitis B Virus RNA Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers have developed a novel diagnostic platform, termed One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA, that provides ultrasensitive, specific, and visual identification of hepatitis B virus RNA. The molecular approach seamlessly combines reverse transcription loop-mediated isothermal amplification with Pyrococcus furiosus Argonaute-mediated identification inside a custom-engineered sealed tube. Hepatitis B virus infection remains a pressing global health concern requiring reliable diagnostic strategies. Circulating hepatitis B virus RNA has been proposed as a valuable surrogate biomarker that directly reflects the transcriptional activity of covalently closed circular DNA within virus-infected hepatocytes. By mirroring intrahepatic covalently closed circular DNA activity, measuring circulating viral RNA aids clinicians in assessing therapeutic efficacy and evaluating antiviral treatment response in infected individuals. To establish the One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA detection system, the investigators designed specific loop-mediated isothermal amplification primers and 5′-phosphorylated guide DNAs targeting hepatitis B virus RNA. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges The research team systematically evaluated and optimized critical diagnostic parameters governing the performance of the molecular assay. These optimization procedures established the precise reaction temperature required for reverse transcription loop-mediated isothermal amplification. Additionally, the investigators systematically calibrated the reaction duration necessary for Pyrococcus furiosus Argonaute-mediated cleavage to ensure reliable target recognition and enzymatic activity. Analytical validation demonstrated that the One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA assay achieved an ultrasensitive limit of detection of five copies per reaction. Furthermore, analytical specificity testing showed that the platform displayed no cross-reactivity against other microbial species, confirming that the designed primers and guide DNAs selectively identify hepatitis B virus RNA without generating false-positive background results. The entire diagnostic workflow was successfully completed within ninety minutes from initial processing to visual result interpretation. This timeline comprises approximately forty-five minutes for hepatitis B virus RNA preparation, thirty minutes of reverse transcription loop-mediated isothermal amplification, ten minutes for Pyrococcus furiosus Argonaute-mediated cleavage, and less than two minutes for visual readout on the gold nanoparticle-based lateral flow biosensor. The clinical feasibility of the diagnostic platform was assessed through serum specimens obtained from chronic hepatitis B patients. When tested on these clinical serum samples, the One-Tube-PfAgo-HBV-RNA assay demonstrated one hundred percent concordance with the standard reference method, quantitative real-time PCR for hepatitis B virus RNA, confirming its clinical diagnostic reliability. Discover more Study Biology Study Chemistry Read Health Guides Lab Equipment Sales Health The authors concluded that these preliminary results demonstrate considerable promise for the accurate and convenient monitoring of chronic hepatitis B infections. Furthermore, the novel closed-tube platform establishes an important technical foundation for developing point-of-care molecular diagnostics targeting other infectious diseases, particularly for healthcare settings in resource-limited regions. Hepatitis B vaccination at birth has fallen sharply among U.S. newborns, including babies at highest risk - Medical Daily

News Brief
A new study reveals a sharp decline in hepatitis B vaccination rates among U.S. newborns, particularly those at highest risk. The Truveta study found that the coverage rate for the hepatitis B birth dose dropped to 65.6%, a trend that began in 2023. This decline raises concerns about the potential impact on infant health, as hepatitis B can lead to chronic infection and serious complications if not addressed early. However, researchers have urged caution, emphasizing the need for further investigation rather than immediate alarm. The report highlights the importance of maintaining vaccination programs to prevent the spread of the virus and protect vulnerable populations. While the data indicate a concerning trend, the study does not provide definitive evidence of increased disease incidence or risk, underscoring the necessity for continued monitoring and targeted interventions.

美国新生儿乙型肝炎病毒携带率下降,公共卫生警报报告表明 - CIDRAP

Hepatitis B vaccination rates in US newborns falling, Public Health Alerts report shows - CIDRAP
新闻简报CIDRAP 公共卫生警报最近的一份报告强调了美国新生儿中乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 发病率下降的情况,令人担忧。研究结果表明,每年有超过 640 名婴儿是由未经检测的乙型肝炎母亲所生,大约 360,000 名婴儿是由未经检测的母亲所生。这一趋势引发了人们对婴儿感染 传播 病毒的可能性的担忧,因为缺乏检测和 Z​​XQTERMJQXZ 可能会使他们变得脆弱。该报告强调需要改进筛查和 疫苗接种 做法,但没有提供具体病例数或 死亡率 比率。该问题目前正在调查中,该报告呼吁公共卫生官员采取行动,解决与这种下降相关的日益增长的风险。

事件

规模、影响与风险

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B vaccination rates in US newborns falling, Public Health Alerts report shows - CIDRAP

News Brief
A recent report by CIDRAP's Public Health Alerts highlights a concerning decline in hepatitis B vaccination rates among newborns in the United States. The findings suggest that over 640 infants annually are born to mothers with hepatitis B who are not tested, and approximately 360,000 babies are born to unscreened mothers. This trend raises concerns about the potential for increased transmission of the virus to infants, as the lack of testing and vaccination could leave them vulnerable. The report underscores the need for improved screening and vaccination practices, but it does not provide specific case counts or mortality rates. The issue is currently under investigation, and the report serves as a call to action for public health officials to address the growing risk associated with this decline.
Original source text (English)
