B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

孟加拉国研究显示乙型肝炎下降,脂肪肝激增 - Bioengineer.org

Fatty Liver Disease Surges as Hepatitis B Declines in Bangladesh Study - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A study conducted at Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib Medical University in Dhaka, Bangladesh, over a ten-year period from 2013 to 2022 revealed a significant shift in the causes of chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, which was the primary cause, decreased from 51.1 percent to 44.4 percent of admissions, while nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD) nearly sevenfold increased from 1.2 percent to 8.1 percent. The research analyzed 4,658 confirmed adult patients with chronic liver disease, finding that cirrhosis was the most common clinical presentation, accounting for 50.10 percent of cases. The study highlights the growing impact of metabolic disorders on liver disease in Bangladesh, with NAFLD becoming a more prominent cause. The findings suggest that the operational burden on tertiary centers is changing due to this shift, but no specific public health interventions were outlined in the report. The study was approved by the university’s Institutional Review Board and used standardized diagnostic criteria throughout the period.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究从2013年到2022年进行了十年,新闻报道于2026年11月23日发布(N1,N68)。
地点与对象
该研究在孟加拉国达卡的 Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib 医科大学进行,涉及 4,658 名确诊患有慢性肝病的成年患者(N2、N16)。
事件
一项回顾性研究发现,乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 患病率 的下降是慢性肝病的主要原因,而非酒精性脂肪肝病 (NAFLD) 则显着增加(N3、N4)。
规模、影响与风险
Chronic hepatitis B accounted for 47.30 percent of admissions (2,201 patients), and NAFLD rose from 1.2 percent to 8.1 percent of admissions. Cirrhosis was the most common clinical presentation, representing 50.10 percent of cases (N23, N28, N30).
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是对医院记录的回顾性分析,获得了伦理批准并使用了标准化的诊断流程。研究结果表明肝病负担发生了可记录的变化,但未详细说明具体的应对措施(N11,N12,N24)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Fatty Liver Disease Surges as Hepatitis B Declines in Bangladesh Study - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted at Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib Medical University in Dhaka, Bangladesh, over a ten-year period from 2013 to 2022 revealed a significant shift in the causes of chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, which was the primary cause, decreased from 51.1 percent to 44.4 percent of admissions, while nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD) nearly sevenfold increased from 1.2 percent to 8.1 percent. The research analyzed 4,658 confirmed adult patients with chronic liver disease, finding that cirrhosis was the most common clinical presentation, accounting for 50.10 percent of cases. The study highlights the growing impact of metabolic disorders on liver disease in Bangladesh, with NAFLD becoming a more prominent cause. The findings suggest that the operational burden on tertiary centers is changing due to this shift, but no specific public health interventions were outlined in the report. The study was approved by the university’s Institutional Review Board and used standardized diagnostic criteria throughout the period.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Fatty Liver Disease Surges as Hepatitis B Declines in Bangladesh Study by November 23, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Fatty Liver Disease Surges as Hepatitis B Declines in Bangladesh Study Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A decade-long retrospective study conducted at Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib Medical University in Dhaka has uncovered a significant transformation in the clinical landscape of chronic liver disease in Bangladesh, demonstrating a marked shift from viral infections toward metabolic pathology. Drawing on hospital registry records spanning from 2013 through 2022, the investigation evaluated 4,658 confirmed adult patients admitted to the specialized inpatient department of hepatology. While chronic infection with hepatitis B virus persisted as the primary single underlying cause of liver disease across the full decade, its relative prevalence fell significantly from 51.1 percent during the initial 2013 to 2016 observation window to 44.4 percent in the subsequent 2017 to 2022 period. Concurrently, nonalcoholic fatty liver disease experienced an extraordinary expansion, ascending nearly sevenfold from 1.2 percent of admissions in the earlier interval to 8.1 percent in the later years. These documented alterations indicate that metabolic complications are swiftly reshaping the operational burden placed upon tertiary centers. The global impact of chronic liver disorders represents an immense public health challenge, accounting for approximately two million deaths worldwide every year. Clinical data indicate that roughly one million of these annual fatalities stem directly from complications and sequelae of liver cirrhosis, while another one million deaths result from viral hepatitis and hepatocellular carcinoma. Within Bangladesh, figures released by the World Health Organization in 2020 established liver disease as the ninth leading contributor to nationwide mortality, responsible for 21,024 deaths and representing 2.94 percent of all registered fatalities. Historically, viral hepatitis B and hepatitis C, coupled in many countries with alcohol misuse, served as the primary causes of cirrhosis. However, the international epidemiology of liver cirrhosis has shifted substantially in recent years, with nonalcoholic fatty liver disease emerging as the leading cause of cirrhosis worldwide due to surging global rates of obesity, insulin resistance, type 2 diabetes, and metabolic syndrome. To investigate these evolving patterns in Bangladesh, researchers analyzed archival hospital records from the Department of Hepatology at Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib Medical University, the primary tertiary referral facility in Dhaka. Ethical approval for the retrospective study was granted by the university’s Institutional Review Board prior to initiation. Between 2013 and 2022, the inpatient hepatology department admitted 10,404 patients. Researchers systematically applied predefined exclusion criteria, removing 5,210 individuals whose diagnoses fell outside the scope of chronic liver disease, including patients with acute viral hepatitis, acute liver failure, or hepatocellular carcinoma occurring without underlying cirrhosis. From the remaining 5,194 records, an additional 536 patients were excluded due to insufficient diagnostic documentation to definitively confirm or exclude chronic liver pathology. Consequently, the final analysis included 4,658 hospitalized adult patients aged eighteen or older who had verifiable clinical, biochemical, or imaging evidence of chronic liver disease spanning the ten-year period. Discover more Soothe Digestion Find Treatments Study Biology Check Symptoms Try Herbal Tea Diagnostic criteria remained standardized throughout the study period. Chronic hepatitis B diagnosis required positive hepatitis B surface antigen for more than six months alongside concordant clinical and biochemical evidence. Chronic hepatitis C was established through positive anti-HCV antibodies and detectable HCV RNA. Nonalcoholic fatty liver disease was defined by ultrasonographic demonstration of hepatic steatosis in the absence of significant alcohol intake, viral hepatitis, or secondary causes, with or without elevated transaminases. Autoimmune hepatitis was identified using international guidelines incorporating clinical findings, elevated serum immunoglobulin G, positive autoantibodies such as ANA or ASMA, and liver biopsy when available. Total anti-HBc or occult hepatitis B required positive anti-HBc with negative surface antigens. Major clinical presentations encompassed decompensated cirrhosis, which represented 50.10 percent of cases, followed by cirrhosis with hepatocellular carcinoma at 24.30 percent, alongside compensated cirrhosis, acute-on-chronic liver failure, and documented cases of recompensated cirrhosis. Diagnostic protocols were preserved consistently across all ten years of hospital intake. Demographic analysis of the 4,658 patients revealed an overall mean age of 49.5 years with a standard deviation of 13.8 years. The largest single demographic bracket comprised individuals aged 50 to 59 years, accounting for 27.7 percent of all admissions, and male patients predominated significantly, constituting 75.5 percent of the hospitalized population. Across the entire cohort, in-hospital mortality was documented at 5.90 percent. Evaluation of the full ten-year etiological distribution established chronic hepatitis B virus infection as the primary cause of chronic liver disease, responsible for 2,201 patients or 47.30 percent of total admissions. Cryptogenic liver disease represented the second most common categorization, accounting for 874 patients or 18.80 percent, followed by chronic hepatitis C virus infection in 504 patients or 10.80 percent. Non-B-non-C etiology comprised 463 patients or 9.90 percent, while nonalcoholic fatty liver disease accounted for 240 patients or 5.20 percent. Total anti-HBc or occult hepatitis B was identified in 198 patients (4.30 percent), and Wilson’s disease accounted for 74 patients (1.60 percent). Discover more Buy Maps Learn Hindi Explore History Infrequent etiologies and dual liver pathologies were also captured within the hospital registry. Alcohol-induced liver disease was identified in only 20 patients, representing 0.40 percent of total admissions, aligning with low consumption rates documented in Bangladesh. Autoimmune hepatitis was diagnosed in 17 patients, also accounting for 0.40 percent, reflecting its recognized rarity across Asia where female-to-male ratios average four to one. Budd-Chiari syndrome was documented in 16 patients (0.30 percent), and dual chronic infection involving both hepatitis B and hepatitis C occurred in 16 patients (0.30 percent). Furthermore, coexisting nonalcoholic fatty liver disease and anti-HBc total was recorded in 11 patients (0.20 percent), combined hepatitis C and anti-HBc total occurred in 10 patients (0.20 percent), and hereditary haemochromatosis was identified in 8 individuals (0.20 percent). Rare dual conditions included three cases of concurrent hepatitis B and fatty liver disease (0.10 percent), alongside single cases combining hepatitis C with fatty liver, hepatitis B with autoimmune hepatitis, and fatty liver with autoimmune hepatitis. To analyze temporal trends, researchers compared the distribution of etiologies between two distinct periods: 2013 to 2016, comprising 1,976 patients, and 2017 to 2022, comprising 2,682 patients. Chi-square analysis demonstrated statistically significant shifts across multiple disease categories. The proportion of patients with chronic hepatitis B infection dropped from 51.1 percent (1,009 patients) to 44.4 percent (1,192 patients), a statistically significant decrease (p < 0.001). Chronic hepatitis C also declined significantly from 11.3 percent (224 patients) to 10.4 percent (280 patients, p = 0.032). Non-B-non-C liver disease decreased from 11.7 percent (232 patients) to 8.6 percent (231 patients, p < 0.001). Conversely, nonalcoholic fatty liver disease experienced a dramatic, statistically significant surge, rising from 1.2 percent (24 patients) in the earlier period to 8.1 percent (216 patients) in the later timeframe (p < 0.001). Occult hepatitis B or anti-HBc total also increased significantly from 3.1 percent (61 patients) to 5.1 percent (137 patients, p = 0.001), while autoimmune hepatitis rose from 0.2 percent (3 patients) to 0.5 percent (14 patients, p = 0.038). Changes in cryptogenic disease (18.3 to 19.1 percent) and alcohol (0.5 to 0.4 percent) lacked statistical significance. Annual longitudinal evaluation across the ten individual years reinforced these clinical shifts. Hepatitis B prevalence decreased steadily from 54.5 percent of admissions in 2013 to 42.0 percent in 2022. Conversely, nonalcoholic fatty liver disease exhibited a striking ascent, expanding from 0.9 percent in 2013 to 14.4 percent of admissions by 2022. Chronic hepatitis C demonstrated a relatively stable overall pattern, registering 8.7 percent in 2013 and 8.8 percent in 2022, following a mild interim peak through 2016. Anti-HBc total or occult hepatitis B rose from 2.8 percent in 2013 to 6.4 percent in 2022, while autoimmune hepatitis shifted from 0.5 percent to 1.8 percent. The authors noted that these trajectories reflect wider international epidemiological shifts, drawing comparisons to United States NHANES survey data between 1988 and 2016 involving 58,731 adults, which documented a rise in fatty liver disease from 20.0 to 31.9 percent alongside a fifty percent decrease in hepatitis C. The etiological shifts reported at the tertiary center align with broader epidemiological developments across Bangladesh and South Asia. The declining proportion of chronic hepatitis B reflects public health gains achieved following the integration of hepatitis B vaccination into Bangladesh’s national Expanded Programme on Immunization in 2003, which successfully lowered transmission rates in younger generations. Simultaneously, direct-acting antiviral treatments have improved clinical outcomes for hepatitis C. In contrast, the rapid expansion of fatty liver disease corresponds with mounting metabolic risk factors across the country. Prior community studies cited by the researchers identified an overall adult fatty liver disease prevalence of 33.86 percent in Bangladesh, with nonalcoholic steatohepatitis affecting 42.4 percent of those patients. In that national research, the odds of developing fatty liver disease were 4.51 times higher among overweight participants and 10.71 times higher among obese individuals, with elevated risks noted among rural females, middle-aged adults, and patients diagnosed with diabetes or hypertension. The investigators emphasized specific methodological limitations that define the study’s interpretive boundaries. As a retrospective single-center evaluation conducted within a tertiary hospital, referral bias represents an inherent constraint, as admitted patients typically present with more severe, symptomatic, or advanced liver complications than individuals managed in outpatient or primary care environments. However, because BSMMU functions as the foremost referral center in Bangladesh, drawing patients from diverse geographical and socioeconomic backgrounds, this institutional reach partially mitigates referral distortion. Additionally, data collection relied upon manually maintained hospital records, introducing potential information bias and missing data, though rigorous eligibility screening and validation were conducted to minimize inaccuracies. The authors concluded that while viral infections remain the predominant cause of chronic liver disease admissions, the rapid rise of nonalcoholic fatty liver disease demands that clinicians, health authorities, and policymakers actively recognize metabolic complications and implement targeted public health strategies to address this expanding burden. The study was conducted over a ten-year period from 2013 to 2022, with the news report published on November 23, 2026 (N1, N68).
Location and population
The study was carried out at Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujib Medical University in Dhaka, Bangladesh, involving 4,658 confirmed adult patients with chronic liver disease (N2, N16).
Event
A retrospective study found a decline in hepatitis B virus (HBV) prevalence as the primary cause of chronic liver disease, while nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD) increased significantly (N3, N4).
Scale, impact and risk
Chronic hepatitis B accounted for 47.30 percent of admissions (2,201 patients), and NAFLD rose from 1.2 percent to 8.1 percent of admissions. Cirrhosis was the most common clinical presentation, representing 50.10 percent of cases (N23, N28, N30).
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was a retrospective analysis of hospital records, with ethical approval and standardized diagnostic protocols. The findings indicate a documented shift in the burden of liver disease, but no specific response measures were detailed (N11, N12, N24).

医生对新生儿乙型肝炎发病率下降发出严重警告 疫苗接种 - 赫芬顿邮报

Doctors Have A Serious Warning About The Decline In Newborn Hepatitis B Vaccinations - HuffPost
新闻简报The article highlights a concerning decline in newborn hepatitis B vaccination rates in the United States, with 65.6% of babies vaccinated as of June 2026, down from 80% in 2022. Doctors warn that this drop could lead to a rise in hepatitis B infections, which can result in chronic disease, liver failure, and liver cancer. The study, conducted by researchers from Callen-Lorde Community Health Center and Truveta, analyzed over 1.3 million babies. The decrease is linked to a Trump-era proposal to remove the hepatitis B vaccine from the childhood schedule, which is currently blocked by an American Academy of Pediatrics lawsuit. Experts emphasize the importance of early vaccination to prevent lifelong complications, as transmission during birth poses the highest risk of chronic infection. The article underscores the potential public health impact of this trend, urging attention to the issue.

Wellness Doctors Have A Serious Warning About The Decline In Newborn Hepatitis B Vaccinations Just 65.6% of babies received the vaccine, compared to 80% in 2022. Doctors say the consequences could be dire. By Jillian Wilson Senior Wellness Reporter, HuffPost Nov 26, 2026, 07:00 AM EST |Updated 10 hours ago Leave a Comment LOADINGERROR LOADING 1/200:12Ravens Star Says Diabetes Helped Him As An Athlete Skip Ad Continue watchingRavens Star Says Diabetes Helped Him As An Athleteafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE Vaccination rates in children and babies are dropping across the United States. This is made clear by current measles outbreaks in states such as Pennsylvania, New York, Wisconsin and more. Now new research, published in the New England Journal of Medicine Evidence, shows that parents are not only opting their babies out of the measles vaccine, but also the hepatitis B vaccine. The study was conducted by researchers from Callen-Lorde Community Health Center in New York and Truveta, a healthcare data company. Advertisement Researchers analyzed the medical records of more than 1.3 million babies and found that as of June 2026, 65.6% of newborn babies were vaccinated against hepatitis B in their first 24 hours of life, down from 80% in 2022. While the drop in hepatitis B vaccination rates started in 2023, the continuing decline comes at a time when Trump-era federal vaccine advisers voted to remove the hepatitis B vaccine from the childhood vaccine schedule. (The move is currently blocked by an American Academy of Pediatrics lawsuit, so the hepatitis B shot still remains part of the schedule.) “I find it unfortunate that vaccination rates are declining, because this inevitably means that rates of hepatitis B infection will ultimately rise, and lives will be permanently affected,” said Dr. Leslie Treece, a pediatrician in Tennessee. Treece is not involved with this study. Advertisement “Prior to 1991, we used to recommend vaccination only to ‘high risk’ groups. In spite of this, there were still about 24,000 cases per year of hepatitis B infection in infants [in the U.S.],” Treece said. “After adopting universal newborn vaccination, the case number dropped dramatically to a few hundred or less cases in the US per year.” Hepatitis B can be lifelong and chronic, and can lead to liver cancer and liver failure. “With hepatitis B, this is especially worrisome because it is a lifelong, chronic disease with no cure, leading to liver failure and liver cancer later in life,” said Dr. Zhen Chan, a pediatrician at Zarminali Pediatrics in Washington, D.C. Chan was not involved with this study. “Both of these diseases can lead to early death,” Chan added. Hepatitis B is not always lifelong and chronic, but it is more likely to be the earlier someone is infected. Advertisement This vaccine is given to newborns because transmission during birth ― or within the first 24 hours of life from an infected mother ― carries the highest risk of chronic hepatitis B infection and later risk of cancer and liver disease, explained Dr. Alexandra Yonts, a pediatric infectious diseases specialist at Children’s National in Washington, D.C. Yonts is not involved with this study. “About 1 in 4 children with lifelong infection eventually die of cirrhosis or liver cancer if untreated. The longer a baby is left unvaccinated, the higher the chance of getting infected,” Chan said. Sollina Images via Getty Images Hepatitis B vaccination rates are declining in newborns in the United States. Advertisement Hepatitis B is rare; it’s estimated that 640,000 adults have chronic hepatitis B, but that further points to the power of vaccination. “Many families will question how their baby would even get hepatitis B when the rates are so low. I usually explain that rates are low because of the vaccine itself, and the lower the vaccination rates in the community, the greater the risk of getting the disease,” Chan said. “The safety data supports giving the hepatitis B vaccine at such an early age. Completing the full series gives a baby about a 98% level protection against the disease,” Chan added. Advertisement Not only does the vaccine prevent babies from infection when they’re young, but it goes on protect against hepatitis B infection when they reach adulthood, said Dr. Rick Malley, a senior physician in pediatrics, division of infectious diseases at Boston Children’s Hospital. Malley is not involved with this study. “The idea of preventive vaccination for diseases that the individual might really only encounter much later in life is one of the great successes of hepatitis B vaccination,” Malley said. “By immunizing children early, we were really preventing ... transmission perinatally, and also providing protection later in life.” The consequences of a decline in hepatitis B vaccination won’t be seen for some time. “There are all these understandable instances of confusion that people might have when they hear their healthcare provider tell them one thing, and then they go on the internet, or they get exposed to some video that tells them exactly the opposite,” said Malley. Advertisement It’s not surprising that people pause the recommended vaccine schedule when they see conflicting messaging online, he noted. “Unfortunately, we know that when that happens, the likelihood that an individual will get a vaccine-preventable disease increases significantly,” Malley said. The current measles outbreaks throughout the U.S. are just one example of this, and the now decline in hepatitis B vaccination is another, he added. “I think now with hepatitis B, the consequences of of these drops in vaccinations ... may not be observed quite as quickly as we, unfortunately, are seeing with the measles outbreaks,” Malley said. “So, that is also confusing in a way because the consequences of this may not be seen for a while.” Advertisement “And I think, broadly, it also points to another bit of a problem in the field of vaccines, which is that ... vaccines are really victims of their own success,” he said. Most people don’t know someone who had polio. Most people had not heard about measles until the current outbreak in the U.S. “Trying to convince the public that the reason they don’t know about these terrifying diseases is precisely because of vaccination is a bit of a hurdle,” he said. In the end, “the benefits of preventing chronic Hep B infection and complications of liver disease and liver cancer far outweigh any minor risks ... of vaccination,” Yonts said, adding that there are no major adverse effects or side effects of the vaccine. Advertisement “Giving this vaccine to every baby ... is the most equitable and responsible thing we can do for our population,” Yonts added. “We shouldn’t make babies (and the future of our country) pay the price of failings of our healthcare for pregnant women.” HELP US HIT 31,000BY DEC. 31 Good journalism changes how we see the world. Hepatitis B Shot at Birth Dips Among Babies of Infected Moms - MedPage Today

News Brief
The news report from MedPage Today, published on November 25, 2026, discusses a decline in the administration of the hepatitis B shot at birth among babies whose mothers are infected with the hepatitis B virus. Researchers emphasize that the birth dose vaccination is a critical safety net for preventing hepatitis B transmission from mother to child. The report highlights the importance of this intervention in reducing the risk of infection in newborns. However, the article does not provide specific details about the scale of the issue, the number of affected babies, or the exact reasons behind the dip in vaccination rates. The report is part of a broader collection of medical news and does not include information on the response status or any uncertainties related to the situation. The findings are based on the implications of the report and are not supported by explicit target-specific methods or results.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

美国婴儿乙型肝炎出生剂量 疫苗接种 降至 65.6% - HCPLive

Hepatitis B Birth Dose Vaccination Falls to 65.6% in US Infants - HCPLive
新闻简报HCPLive的文章报道,美国婴儿中乙型肝炎出生剂量疫苗接种的比率已下降至65.6%。该信息于 11 小时前发布,凸显了免疫接种工作中令人担忧的趋势。该报告没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或与下降相关的风险水平。它还没有提及卫生当局采取的任何调查或应对措施。该证据基于新闻摘要,没有进一步的细节。疫苗接种 率的下降可能表明需要采取新的公共卫生举措,以确保新生儿免受乙型肝炎的充分保护。
查看新闻五要素
时间
11 小时前 作者:Cosby A.
地点与对象
美国,婴儿。
事件
据 HCPLive 报道,美国婴儿乙型肝炎出生剂量 疫苗接种 覆盖率已降至 65.6%。
规模、影响与风险
美国婴儿乙型肝炎出生剂量的 疫苗接种 率为 65.6%,表明免疫工作有所下降。证据中没有提及具体病例、死亡或风险水平。
应对、状态与不确定性
该报告强调了 疫苗接种 比率的下降,但没有提供有关应对措施或调查状态的详细信息。该证据基于新闻摘要,没有进一步的细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B Birth Dose Vaccination Falls to 65.6% in US Infants - HCPLive

News Brief
The article from HCPLive reports that the rate of Hepatitis B birth dose vaccination in the United States has dropped to 65.6% among infants. This information was published 11 hours ago and highlights a concerning trend in immunization efforts. The report does not provide specific case counts, deaths, or risk levels associated with the decline. It also does not mention any investigation or response measures taken by health authorities. The evidence is based on a news summary, and no further details are available. The decline in vaccination rates may indicate a need for renewed public health initiatives to ensure adequate protection against Hepatitis B in newborns.

The vaccination rate for Hepatitis B birth dose in US infants is 65.6%, indicating a decline in immunization efforts. No specific cases, deaths, or risk levels are mentioned in the evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
The report highlights the decline in vaccination rates but does not provide details on the response measures or investigation status. The evidence is based on a news summary, and no further details are available.

研究发现,免疫抑制群体的乙型肝炎再激活风险有所不同 - Bioengineer.org

Hepatitis B Reactivation Risk Differs Across Immunosuppressed Groups, Study Finds - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 26 日发表的一项研究发现,乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 再激活的风险在免疫功能低下的患者群体中存在显着差异。该研究结合了对现有系统评价的总体回顾和对初步研究的从头荟萃分析,揭示乙型肝炎表面抗原(HBsAg)阳性的个体再激活风险最高,为 11.44%(95% CI:9.17-13.86%),而感染已解决的个体(HBsAg 阴性和抗 HBc 阳性)的风险较低,为 0.99%(95% CI:95% CI: 0.41–1.74%)。研究结果强调需要量身定制的 抗病毒药物 预防策略,因为该研究指出了方法学局限性和亚组间显着的统计异质性。尽管存在这些不确定性,但该研究强调了在免疫抑制人群中管理乙型肝炎病毒再激活的重要性,特别是那些接受免疫抑制治疗或生物制剂的人群。该研究发表在《BMC InfectiousDiseases》上,强调需要进一步研究以阐明不同 抗病毒药物 药物和预防方法的有效性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B Reactivation Risk Differs Across Immunosuppressed Groups, Study Finds - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 26, 2026, by Bioengineer.org found that the risk of hepatitis B virus (HBV) reactivation varies significantly among immunocompromised patient groups. The research combined an umbrella review of existing systematic reviews with a de novo meta-analysis of primary studies, revealing that individuals positive for hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) had the highest reactivation risk at 11.44% (95% CI: 9.17–13.86%), while those with resolved infection (negative HBsAg and positive anti-HBc) had a lower risk of 0.99% (95% CI: 0.41–1.74%). The findings highlight the need for tailored antiviral prophylaxis strategies, as the study noted methodological limitations and significant statistical heterogeneity across subgroups. Despite these uncertainties, the research underscores the importance of managing HBV reactivation in immunosuppressed populations, particularly those undergoing immunosuppressive therapy or biologics. The study was published in BMC Infectious Diseases and emphasizes the ongoing need for further research to clarify the effectiveness of different antiviral agents and prophylactic approaches.

The investigation combined an umbrella review of existing systematic reviews with a de novo meta-analysis of duplicate-free primary studies to clarify reactivation risks that clinicians encounter during immunosuppressive therapy. Hepatitis B virus reactivation represents a preventable yet inconsistently managed complication for individuals undergoing immunosuppressive regimens. While routine antiviral prophylaxis is widely recommended for surface-antigen-positive patients, clinical uncertainty and questions regarding real-world applicability have persisted for patients with resolved infection, those receiving emerging immunotherapies or biologics, solid organ or stem cell transplant recipients, and diverse regional patient populations. To evaluate the existing body of evidence, the research team deployed a structured two-level analytical framework. The first level conducted a review-level synthesis, systematically searching six comprehensive research databases for systematic reviews and meta-analyses published between January 2014 and March 2025. Investigators formally appraised the methodological quality of the retrieved reviews using the AMSTAR-2 tool and calculated the degree of overlap among reviews using the Corrected Covered Area metric. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges The second level of the framework consisted of a de novo meta-analysis directly aggregating data from primary clinical studies identified across the search results. After removing duplicate entries across overlapping reviews, investigators extracted data from 275 primary studies and applied a random-effects model to compute pooled incidence estimates. Findings across both tiers were subsequently assembled into structured evidence maps categorized by serological markers and specific drug exposures. Across the ten included systematic reviews and meta-analyses, the authors identified critical methodological limitations, noting that the overall methodological quality of the review-level evidence was low or very low. A qualitative narrative synthesis of this literature demonstrated that the observed risk of viral reactivation was highest among individuals positive for hepatitis B surface antigen, followed by individuals displaying a surface-antigen-negative and core-antibody-positive serological profile. The subsequent de novo meta-analysis of the 275 original primary studies provided specific pooled quantitative risk estimates across these distinct serological groups. Among individuals who were surface-antigen positive, the pooled incidence of hepatitis B reactivation was 11.44 percent, with a 95 percent confidence interval spanning from 9.17 percent to 13.86 percent. In contrast, patients with resolved infection defined by negative surface antigen and positive core antibody status demonstrated a pooled reactivation incidence of 0.99 percent, with a 95 percent confidence interval from 0.41 percent to 1.74 percent. Additional subgroup evaluations within the resolved hepatitis B cohort indicated that patients lacking surface antibodies experienced higher reactivation estimates than those with detectable surface antibodies. The data also confirmed that prophylactic antiviral therapy was associated with a lower incidence of viral reactivation among surface-antigen-positive patients undergoing immunosuppressive treatment. Discover more Study Biology Study Chemistry Read Health Guides Lab Equipment Sales Health The investigators emphasized significant statistical heterogeneity across all conducted subgroup analyses, cautioning that these summary figures reflect an inherently diverse evidence base rather than exact incidence rates directly generalizable to every bedside setting. Furthermore, direct comparative evidence comparing different antiviral agents remains insufficient, as does conclusive evidence demonstrating prophylactic benefit in patients with resolved infection, highlighting the ongoing need for targeted research. 意大利医院研究发现青少年 疫苗接种 后乙型肝炎抗体水平更高 - Bioengineer.org

Italian Hospital Study Finds Higher Hepatitis B Antibodies Following Adolescent Vaccination - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报意大利罗马 Tor Vergata 大学医院进行的一项研究发现,与婴儿时期接种疫苗的人相比,青少年时期根据强制性乙型肝炎计划接种疫苗的成年人的抗体水平明显更高。该研究分析了 2019 年至 2024 年的医疗记录,结果显示,76.89% 的青少年 队列研究 达到了每升 10 个国际单位或更高的保护滴度,而婴儿疫苗接种组中只有 55.94% 达到了这一水平。尽管存在这些差异,但活动性感染仍然很少,这凸显了国家免疫战略的有效性。2023 年的 监测 数据显示,欧盟和欧洲经济区共有 37,766 例乙型肝炎病例,其中 6.3% 被归类为急性乙型肝炎病例。该研究强调了青少年 疫苗接种 在维持免疫力方面的重要性,尽管其局限性包括关注意大利出生的个体以及一些实验室结果中潜在的误报。研究结果表明,意大利实施的双轨疫苗接种政策有助于长期血清学保护。

Tor Vergata 大学医院的一项回顾性横断面研究发现,与婴儿时期接种疫苗的意大利出生成年人相比,青少年时期接种疫苗的意大利出生成年人的乙型肝炎表面抗体中值浓度显着更高,保护滴度 患病率 更高。

应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项回顾性横断面调查,具有严格的重复数据删除和纳入标准。研究发现,在两代疫苗接种者中,活跃的病毒感染仍然很少见,这凸显了免疫策略的成功。然而,该分析仅限于意大利出生的个体,一些实验室结果显示出不一致,表明潜在的分析干扰或误报。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Italian Hospital Study Finds Higher Hepatitis B Antibodies Following Adolescent Vaccination - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted at Tor Vergata University Hospital in Rome, Italy, found that adults vaccinated as adolescents under a mandatory hepatitis B program had significantly higher antibody levels compared to those vaccinated as infants. The research, which analyzed medical records from 2019 to 2024, revealed that 76.89% of the adolescent cohort achieved protective titers of ten international units per liter or greater, while only 55.94% of the infant-vaccinated group did so. Despite these differences, active infections remained rare, underscoring the effectiveness of national immunization strategies. Surveillance data from 2023 indicated 37,766 hepatitis B cases across the EU and EEA, with 6.3% classified as acute. The study highlights the importance of adolescent vaccination in maintaining immunity, though limitations include the focus on Italian-born individuals and potential false positives in some lab results. The findings suggest that the dual-track vaccination policy implemented in Italy has contributed to long-term serological protection.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Italian Hospital Study Finds Higher Hepatitis B Antibodies Following Adolescent Vaccination by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 7 mins read 0 Italian Hospital Study Finds Higher Hepatitis B Antibodies Following Adolescent Vaccination Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A large retrospective cross-sectional investigation conducted at Tor Vergata University Hospital in Rome has revealed substantial differences in long-term circulating antibody levels across successive national hepatitis B vaccination cohorts in Italy. Evaluating medical and laboratory records from January 1, 2019, through April 30, 2024, clinical investigators found that Italian-born adults who reached eligibility under an adolescent vaccination mandate maintained significantly higher median concentrations of hepatitis B surface antibodies and exhibited a greater prevalence of protective titers than adults vaccinated under a universal infant immunization schedule. Across the evaluated population, individuals belonging to the adolescent vaccination cohort born between 1979 and 1990 achieved protective antibody levels of ten international units per liter or greater in 76.89 percent of cases, whereas those born from 1991 onward under the universal infant policy demonstrated protective titers in 55.94 percent of tested individuals. Despite these divergence patterns in circulating surface antibodies, active viral infections remained rare across both vaccinated generations, underscoring the enduring public health success of nationwide immunization strategies. Surveillance figures compiled by the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control from 2023 indicated that 37,766 cases of hepatitis B virus infection were reported across European Union and European Economic Area nations, translating to a crude notification rate of 8.1 cases per 100,000 population. Approximately 2,400 of these reported cases, representing 6.3 percent of notifications, were classified as acute infections, though surveillance specialists routinely emphasize that acute transmissions are heavily underdiagnosed due to the asymptomatic nature of early clinical stages. Among acute cases with complete transmission information, heterosexual contact represented the most frequently identified transmission route at 18.0 percent, followed closely by nosocomial exposure at 16.0 percent and transmission among men who have sex with men at 14.7 percent. While global preventive campaigns have lowered the prevalence of hepatitis B surface antigen to below one percent in children under five years of age, incomplete birth-dose uptake and an absence of systematic catch-up mechanisms continue to sustain vulnerability windows in multiple international regions. Italy addressed this public health challenge through pioneering legislative intervention by enacting Law Number 165 on May 27, 1991, which established mandatory hepatitis B immunization for all infants at three months of age as an integral component of the national immunization program. Uniquely, the statutory framework simultaneously mandated vaccination for twelve-year-old adolescents through the year 2004, thereby creating a transitional dual-track policy designed to rapidly protect emerging cohorts entering adulthood. Consequently, individuals born in Italy from 1979 onward were systematically covered by statutory vaccination mandates implemented during either early adolescence or infancy. Official programmatic assessments indicate that following the introduction of the national program, approximately 112 million children were immunized, with adolescent vaccination coverage reaching 93.6 percent across targeted communities. For birth cohorts eligible for universal infant vaccination from 1991 onward, joint estimates by the World Health Organization and UNICEF reported third-dose vaccination coverage consistently spanning between 93 percent and 97 percent depending on the specific birth year examined. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges The immune response elicited by hepatitis B surface antigen vaccines characteristically triggers the production of circulating antibodies directed against the surface antigen, designated as anti-HBs. Standard clinical and regulatory thresholds define a concentration of ten international units per liter or milli-international units per milliliter as the benchmark indicative of serological protection against infection. Circulating titers falling below this ten-unit threshold represent uncertain immunity under standard clinical interpretations, generating ongoing debate regarding the persistence of immune memory and the necessity of booster administrations. Although observational evidence demonstrates that infant immunization confers durable protection against clinically significant illness and current guidelines do not recommend routine booster doses for immunocompetent individuals, the absence of randomized clinical trials and the variability of international schedules complicate long-term assessments. Furthermore, approximately ten percent of vaccinated individuals fail to develop protective antibody levels after primary series completion, entering sexual debut with uncertain seroprotection. Healthcare personnel and specific high-risk cohorts continue to receive booster guidance when antibody concentrations drop below ten units, while a dedicated Cochrane systematic review confirmed that broader evidence remains insufficient to firmly support or refute the general necessity of booster doses. To evaluate these long-term serological profiles under real-world conditions, the research team analyzed retrospective diagnostic records from Tor Vergata University Hospital, a high-volume tertiary referral center in Rome. The investigators compiled 83,834 raw hepatitis B testing entries collected between January 1, 2019, and April 30, 2024, subsequently executing rigorous deduplication and applying strict inclusion criteria. Eligible participants were required to be older than 18 years at the time of their first laboratory encounter and born in Italy, an exclusion criterion adopted to prevent confounding from disparate international vaccination policies, differing vaccine products, or unverified foreign vaccination histories. When patients presented multiple serial laboratory encounters across the five-year evaluation window, only the earliest recorded sample was retained for analysis. The resulting core study cohort encompassed 38,049 adult individuals, comprising 17,621 males representing 46.31 percent of the population, 20,211 females accounting for 53.12 percent, and 217 individuals with unavailable sex classifications totaling 0.56 percent. The overall median patient age was 44 years, spanning an age range from 18 to 100 years with an interquartile range of 29 to 62 years, with 91.17 percent originating from Central Italy, 7.29 percent from Southern Italy, and 1.53 percent from Northern Italy. Eligible laboratory profiles required concurrent quantitative anti-HBs measurements alongside qualitative or quantitative hepatitis B surface antigen determinations and qualitative hepatitis B core antibody assessments, with hepatitis B e-antigen and viral DNA results incorporated when available. All serological assays were conducted utilizing Abbott chemiluminescent microparticle immunoassays, while viral nucleic acid quantifications were executed using Abbott real-time polymerase chain reaction platforms. Because hospital databases lacked individual vaccination cards, the investigators could not directly document patient-level ages at vaccination, specific manufacturer formulations, exact dose counts, intervals between doses, or primary post-immunization peak responses. Consequently, the researchers utilized defined birth cohorts as analytical proxies for exposure to national statutory immunization policies. Participants were initially stratified into three broad policy groups: Group A, comprising 20,225 adults born before January 1, 1979, who preceded mandatory vaccination policies; Group B, containing 7,816 individuals born between January 1, 1979, and December 31, 1990, eligible for mandatory adolescent immunization; and Group C, encompassing 10,008 individuals born from January 1, 1991, onward, targeted by universal infant immunization. Initial baseline characterization of the entire 38,049-participant cohort prior to secondary serostatus exclusions illustrated striking epidemiological contrasts among the chronological groups. Among the 20,225 individuals born prior to 1979, 54.4 percent were male, the median anti-HBs titer was 0.44 international units per liter with an interquartile range of 0 to 19.7, and 12,033 of 16,820 individuals with available quantitative titers, or 71.5 percent, exhibited antibody levels below ten units. In contrast, the 7,816 individuals in the 1979 to 1990 adolescent policy group demonstrated a median anti-HBs titer of 84.3 units with an interquartile range of 14.3 to 393.4, and only 20.9 percent exhibited titers below ten units. The 10,008 individuals born from 1991 onward under the universal infant policy showed an intermediate profile, recording a median anti-HBs titer of 14.9 units with an interquartile range of 2.4 to 75.4, while 43.4 percent presented titers below ten units. Because adults born before 1979 had no exposure to universal statutory vaccination and lacked individual records to distinguish sporadic private immunization from natural resolved infections, Group A was excluded from subsequent comparative modeling. To evaluate immunological standing accurately, investigators categorized eligible individuals from Groups B and C into four distinct serological classifications based on combined biomarker panels. Group 1 comprised 100 individuals exhibiting active hepatitis B infection, verified through surface antigen, e-antigen, or viral DNA positivity. Group 2 included 65 individuals with serological evidence of resolved past infection, defined by positive core antibodies in the absence of surface antigen or viral DNA. Group 3 contained 2,757 non-immune individuals displaying negative core antibody findings alongside confirmed absence of surface antigen, e-antigen, and viral DNA, paired with anti-HBs titers below ten units. Group 4 comprised 5,115 protected individuals who maintained anti-HBs concentrations of ten international units per liter or greater without any serological marker of prior or active infection. Within this protected cohort, the 2,611 individuals from the 1979 to 1990 adolescent cohort exhibited a median anti-HBs titer of 156.1 units with an interquartile range of 46.5 to 572.6, whereas the 2,504 protected individuals from the 1991 infant cohort exhibited a median titer of 52.5 units with an interquartile range of 22.5 to 177.0 units. Nonparametric Mann-Whitney analysis confirmed this difference was highly statistically significant at p less than 0.001, with Cliff’s delta metric of 0.33 establishing a moderate effect size. Temporal analyses examining antibody persistence over elapsed time revealed consistent disparities between the two immunization cohorts. Across all evaluation points spanning 17 to 29 years following the age of mandated policy administration, individuals from the 1979 to 1990 adolescent cohort maintained significantly higher median antibody levels than those vaccinated in infancy under the 1991 policy. At the seventeen-year post-eligibility milestone, adults in the adolescent vaccination cohort displayed a median anti-HBs concentration of 56.8 international units per liter with an interquartile range of 11.9 to 438.7, whereas individuals in the infant vaccination cohort demonstrated a median concentration of only 3.5 units with an interquartile range of 0.8 to 17.8. Box-plot distributions confirmed that median titers in the adolescent cohort remained superior across consecutive annual intervals until thirty years post-mandate, at which juncture between-group statistical differences ceased to reach significance. Multivariable logistic regression adjusting for age and sex corroborated that cohort assignment exerted an independent effect on maintaining protective antibody levels of ten units or greater, with the 1979 to 1990 cohort exhibiting an adjusted odds ratio of 1.21 compared to the 1991 cohort. Older age at testing also correlated with higher odds of protective levels with an adjusted odds ratio of 1.08 per additional year, while male sex was associated with lower odds of protection at an adjusted odds ratio of 0.89. In their clinical appraisal, the authors noted that among 65 patients presenting positive qualitative surface antigen results, 31 individuals, or 47.7 percent, simultaneously exhibited anti-HBs titers of ten units or higher. Five of these patients displayed discordant laboratory results characterized by positive qualitative surface antigen but undetectable quantitative antigen testing, pointing toward potential analytical interferences, differing assay thresholds, or unconfirmed false-positive qualitative readings. Biological explanations for concurrent surface antigen and surface antibody positivity include viral surface gene mutations that modify recognized epitopes, infection with heterologous viral genotypes, or antibody generation during active chronic carriage without complete antigen clearance. Importantly, the authors stressed that circulating antibody concentrations constitute only one facet of adaptive immunity and do not directly quantify cellular immune memory or anamnestic T-cell responsiveness. Extensive epidemiological literature demonstrates that infant immunization confers robust, enduring defense against clinical disease even after circulating antibody levels wane beneath detection limits, because preserved memory cells mount rapid anamnestic defenses upon pathogen exposure, rendering breakthrough infections predominantly asymptomatic and rarely chronic. The study authors highlighted several critical methodological limitations inherent to their retrospective single-center design that preclude drawing definitive recommendations regarding booster immunization. The primary constraint stems from the complete absence of patient-level vaccination records, which prevented direct verification of immunization dates, completed dose counts, vaccine formulations, or initial post-vaccination seroconversion. Consequently, the research measured antibody seroprevalence across policy cohorts serving as structural proxies rather than measuring true biological vaccine efficacy. Furthermore, the cross-sectional sampling architecture captured laboratory markers at a single static point in time, precluding longitudinal follow-up, direct measurements of memory B-cell or T-cell recall, or experimental assessments of anamnestic responses following booster challenges. The tertiary hospital setting also introduces potential selection bias, as laboratory testing occurred opportunistically during routine clinical care rather than through randomized population sampling. Finally, restricting the analysis exclusively to Italian-born individuals limits the direct generalizability of these serological proportions to foreign-born residents and migrant populations residing within Italy. 接种乙型肝炎疫苗的新生儿减少 - WESH

Fewer newborns getting vaccinated against hepatitis B - WESH
新闻简报《NEJM Evidence》杂志上发表的一项新研究显示,美国新生儿的乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 发病率显着下降,2026 年上半年降至 65.6%,比 2020 年下降了近 15%。这种下降始于 2023 年,美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 在 12 月停止推荐所有新生儿使用 疫苗 之前2025 年。研究指出,即使在乙型肝炎检测呈阳性的母亲所生的婴儿中,疫苗接种 率也从 97% 左右下降到 93.9%。作者认为,这一下降反映了家长中持续存在的 疫苗 犹豫和困惑,可能是受到 CDC 修订后的指南以及特朗普政府对儿童 疫苗接种 建议的更广泛变化的影响。美国儿科学会继续推荐出生时使用 疫苗,但公共信息的转变导致了不确定性。该报告强调了 疫苗接种 率降低的潜在公共卫生风险,因为乙型肝炎可能导致终生健康问题,包括慢性感染和肝损伤。研究结果强调需要对医疗干预进行清晰的沟通和信任,以维持 疫苗接种 的依从性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道发布于2026年11月26日。研究表明,疫苗接种 比率从 2023 年开始下降,2026 年上半年下降至 65.6%。
地点与对象
人口,特别是新生婴儿,是焦点。该研究提到美国新生儿的 疫苗接种 率下降。
事件
《NEJM Evidence》杂志上发表的一项新研究发现,美国新生儿的乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 率在 2026 年上半年下降至 65.6%,比 2020 年下降了近 15%。这一下降发生在 CDC 于 2025 年 12 月结束对所有新生儿出生时接受 疫苗 的建议之后。
规模、影响与风险
研究报告称,新生儿的 疫苗接种 率从平均 80%(2020-2022 年)下降到 2026 年上半年的 65.6%。甚至在乙型肝炎检测呈阳性的母亲所生的婴儿中也观察到这种下降,从大约 97% 下降到 93.9%。作者认为,这可能表明对建议之外的医疗干预措施的不信任。
应对、状态与不确定性
疾病预防控制中心对乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 指南的改变是在特朗普政府对儿童 疫苗接种 建议进行更广泛的改变之际发生的。该研究强调了公众话语和 疫苗 犹豫不决对 疫苗接种 率下降的影响。作者将这种下降归因于混乱和不信任,但确切原因仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Fewer newborns getting vaccinated against hepatitis B - WESH

News Brief
A new study published in the journal NEJM Evidence reveals a significant decline in hepatitis B vaccination rates for newborns in the U.S., dropping to 65.6% in the first half of 2026—a nearly 15% decrease from 2020. This decline began in 2023, before the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) stopped recommending the vaccine for all newborns in December 2025. The study notes that even among babies born to mothers who tested positive for hepatitis B, vaccination rates fell from around 97% to 93.9%. The authors suggest this drop reflects ongoing vaccine hesitancy and confusion among parents, possibly influenced by the CDC's revised guidelines and broader changes to childhood vaccination recommendations under the Trump administration. The American Academy of Pediatrics continues to recommend the vaccine at birth, but the shift in public messaging has led to uncertainty. The report highlights the potential public health risks of reduced vaccination rates, as hepatitis B can cause lifelong health problems, including chronic infection and liver damage. The findings underscore the need for clear communication and trust in medical interventions to maintain vaccination adherence.
Original source text (English)
Less than two-thirds of newborns got hepatitis B shots in first half of 2026 Watch: Why some parents aren't giving their children the ... Why some parents aren't giving their children the Hep B vaccine WCVB US LIVE Current Time 0:00 / Total Duration 2:27 Sorry, this video is not available, please check back later. Advertisement Updated: 7:08 PM EST Nov 25, 2026 Editorial Standards i Livia Daggett Digital Curator Less than two-thirds of newborns got hepatitis B shots in first half of 2026 Updated: 7:08 PM EST Nov 25, 2026 Editorial Standards i Livia Daggett Digital Curator Less than two-thirds of newborn babies in the U.S. were vaccinated against hepatitis B this year, a drop of nearly 15% from 2020, according to a new study. In a report published this week in the journal NEJM Evidence, researchers found that after reaching an average of 80% from 2020 to 2022, rates of vaccination against hepatitis B for newborns began to decline in 2023, falling to 65.6% in 2026. Advertisement Past video coverage of related study on drop in vaccination rates above. This is the first look at hepatitis B birth vaccination rates after the Centers for Disease Control in December 2025 ended its longstanding recommendation that all babies get the vaccine at birth. Hepatitis B is a virus that can damage the liver and lead to lifelong health problems. The vaccine against it protects against short-term illness and a life-threatening infection called chronic hepatitis B, which is the most common form among newborns. Babies can be exposed to hepatitis B from their mothers, during labor or in the early days of their lives. People often don’t know they are infected with the virus, making it more difficult to prevent transmission. In December 2025, the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices, a committee within the CDC that earlier that year had its members replaced with new ones, stopped recommending HBV vaccination for all newborns. It instead recommended parents of babies whose mothers test negative for hepatitis B make a decision with their healthcare providers. The vaccines were still recommended for babies whose mothers tested positive or had an unknown status. CDC panel rolls back hepatitis B vaccine recommendation for newborns “The bottom line is confusion creates inaction among parents,” Dr. Demetre Daskalakis, an author of the study and a former leader at the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, told the Wall Street Journal . “It’s as clear as mud what to do because you have public messaging that sheds doubt on the effectiveness of the hepatitis B vaccine.” The change in guidance for hepatitis B vaccination came amid sweeping changes to childhood vaccination recommendations pursued by the Trump administration. Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. has a history of sowing doubt about vaccines, and after taking office, he dismissed the members of the CDC’s vaccine policy committee and appointed new ones. And in January 2026, the CDC cut its list of vaccines recommended for every child from 17 to 11, one of the biggest changes to childhood disease prevention in decades. However, this study found that hepatitis B vaccination rates for newborns started to drop in 2023, before the CDC’s change, though the decrease continued afterward. The authors said the continuing drop likely reflects “continuing vaccine hesitancy and confusion among parents” after the changes to newborn hepatitis B vaccine recommendations. A separate study on newborn hepatitis B vaccinations from earlier this year pointed to the decline coinciding with a “period of heightened public discourse in the US regarding childhood vaccination following the COVID-19 pandemic.” The American Academy of Pediatrics, the largest professional association of pediatricians in the U.S., continues to recommend that babies receive the shot at birth. American Academy of Pediatrics releases updated children's vaccination recommendations According to a 2024 CDC report , among children born in the U.S. from 1994 to 2023, routine childhood vaccinations will have prevented approximately 508 million lifetime cases of illness, 32 million hospitalizations and 1,129,000 deaths, as well as resulting in direct savings of $540 billion and societal savings of $2.7 trillion. During that same time period, the hepatitis B vaccine that the CDC no longer recommends for all children prevented 6 million cases of hepatitis B and 940,000 hospitalizations, the CDC report said. The report found that hepatitis B birth vaccine doses fell even among babies born to mothers who did test positive for hepatitis B, dropping from an average of around 97% to 98% in 2018-2025 to 93.9% during the first half of 2026. The authors said that drop could “potentially indicate distrust of medical interventions even beyond recommendations.” Get the Facts: The US overhauled its childhood vaccination schedule. Here's how What does Trump's order change about childhood vaccine guidelines? SCARF2 帮助 乙型肝炎病毒 贩运和释放

SCARF2 Aids Hepatitis B Virus Trafficking and Release
新闻简报Xia & He Publishing Inc. 最近在《临床与转化肝病学杂志》上发表的一项研究将 SCARF2 确定为 宿主 因子,可促进 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 的细胞内运输和核衣壳释放。研究表明,SCARF2 与 HBV 颗粒相互作用,特别是大包膜蛋白的 preS1 区域,其缺失可显着降低 HepG2-NTCP 和 HepaRG 细胞中的 HBV 感染。这一发现有助于了解 HBV 感染的进入后事件,这对于制定新的 抗病毒药物 策略至关重要。该研究表明,针对 SCARF2 可以预防新发感染并限制持续性 HBV 感染,尤其是在新感染的肝细胞中。然而,研究结果仍处于研究阶段,需要进一步的研究来验证这些结果并评估潜在治疗干预措施的安全性。SCARF2 也被认为是未来 抗病毒药物 开发的潜在目标,其策略侧重于肝脏靶向递送、短暂早期抑制或选择性破坏 SCARF2-HBV 相互作用。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

SCARF2 Aids Hepatitis B Virus Trafficking and Release

News Brief
A recent study published in the Journal of Clinical and Translational Hepatology, by Xia & He Publishing Inc., identifies SCARF2 as a host factor that facilitates the intracellular trafficking and nucleocapsid release of the Hepatitis B virus (HBV). The research shows that SCARF2 interacts with HBV particles, particularly the preS1 region of the large envelope protein, and its depletion significantly reduces HBV infection in HepG2-NTCP and HepaRG cells. This discovery contributes to understanding the post-entry events of HBV infection, which are critical for developing new antiviral strategies. The study suggests that targeting SCARF2 could prevent de novo infection and limit persistent HBV infection, especially in newly infected hepatocytes. However, the findings are still in the research phase, and further studies are needed to validate these results and assess the safety of potential therapeutic interventions. SCARF2 is also noted as a potential target for future antiviral development, with strategies focusing on liver-targeted delivery, transient early inhibition, or selective disruption of the SCARF2–HBV interaction.
Original source text (English)
Mirage News Mirage News Mirage News Science 27 Nov 2026 2:14 am AEDT Date Time Xia & He Publishing Inc. Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection affects approximately 257.5 million people worldwide and causes cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma. Functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) is defined by sustained serum HBsAg loss, with or without anti-HBs seroconversion. Unlike hepatitis C, CHB remains difficult to cure: current antivirals and immunomodulators suppress replication but rarely clear HBsAg. After hepatocyte entry, HBV delivers relaxed circular DNA to the nucleus, where it forms covalently closed circular DNA (cccDNA), the transcriptional template for viral replication and antigen synthesis. cccDNA and integrated HBV DNA are major barriers to cure. Despite decades of research, post-entry events—intracellular trafficking, nucleocapsid uncoating, and nuclear import—remain incompletely understood. HBV entry is a multistep process. Virions first bind hepatocyte heparan sulfate proteoglycans via the S domain, then the large surface protein preS1 domain binds sodium taurocholate cotransporting polypeptide (NTCP), triggering internalization. NTCP variants may alter susceptibility. Endocytosis depends on NTCP ubiquitination and epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR) signaling. Internalized virions are transported toward the nucleus, but the mechanisms governing post-entry trafficking and nucleocapsid release are unclear. Identification of SCARF2 Scavenger receptor class F member 2 (SCARF2) is a scavenger receptor family member; homozygous mutations cause Van den Ende–Gupta syndrome. Li et al. identified SCARF2 as an intracellular receptor that transports HBV-containing endosomes to nuclear pore complexes (NPCs) and promotes nucleocapsid release. In a targeted screen of hepatocyte membrane proteins, SCARF2 depletion markedly reduced HBV infection in HepG2-NTCP and HepaRG cells, whereas overexpression increased susceptibility. SCARF2 acted specifically during early infection, with minimal effect once infection was established. Mechanistically, SCARF2 directly interacts with HBV particles. Its extracellular EGF-like domains 4–6 bind the preS1 region of the HBV large envelope protein, with amino acids 69–108 as the major binding region; residue 90 is critical, as mutation impaired binding and reduced infectivity. Imaging showed that internalized HBV enters SCARF2-containing endosomes, which move to the perinuclear region and accumulate near NPCs. Viral particles are enriched on the cytoplasmic side of NPCs, and SCARF2 loss impairs this accumulation. SCARF2 also promotes nucleocapsid release from endosomes; knockdown reduced release efficiency, limiting rcDNA delivery to the nucleus and cccDNA formation. Thus, SCARF2 acts downstream of NTCP, coupling endosomal trafficking with nucleocapsid release to support productive infection. Together with NTCP, this supports a sequential entry model: HSPG attachment, NTCP engagement via preS1, EGFR-dependent endocytosis, and SCARF2-directed trafficking and nucleocapsid release. SCARF2 as a Potential Therapeutic Target SCARF2 may be a host target for preventing de novo HBV infection. Because it contributes indirectly to cccDNA formation in newly infected hepatocytes, targeting SCARF2 could inhibit infection downstream of NTCP internalization and limit persistent infection. Benefit may be greatest for preventing de novo infection, limiting spread to uninfected hepatocytes, and reducing reinfection after cccDNA clearance. SCARF2 genetic variation may influence susceptibility, persistence, and treatment outcomes, analogous to the NTCP SLC10A1 S267F variant associated with HBV resistance. Systematic genetic and epidemiological studies are needed. The NTCP entry inhibitor bulevirtide provides proof of concept for host-targeted antivirals. SCARF2-targeting approaches—small-molecule inhibitors or agents disrupting the SCARF2–preS1 interaction—may prevent de novo infection and block cccDNA establishment. Safety requires attention: SCARF2 is essential in development, and homozygous mutations cause Van den Ende–Gupta syndrome. Strategies should focus on liver-targeted delivery, transient early inhibition, or selective disruption of the SCARF2–HBV interaction. SCARF2 may also inform HBV animal models, because the late entry block in murine hepatocytes expressing human NTCP may involve species-specific SCARF2 differences. Conclusions Li et al. identify SCARF2 as a host factor mediating HBV intracellular trafficking and nucleocapsid release. By showing how SCARF2 transports HBV-containing endosomes to NPCs, the study advances understanding of post-entry events and refines models of HBV infection. The SCARF2–HBV interaction is a potential target for future antiviral development. JCTH has established high standards for publication of original research, which are characterized by a study's novelty, quality, and ethical conduct in the scientific process as well as in the communication of the research findings. Each issue includes articles by leading authorities on topics in hepatology that are germane to the most current challenges in the field. Special features include reports on the latest advances in drug development and technology that are relevant to liver diseases. REG - GSK PLC - Hibsago 在中国获批 慢性乙型肝炎 - TradingView

REG - GSK PLC - Hibsago approved in China for chronic hepatitis B - TradingView
新闻简报On 26 November 2026, China’s National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) in combination with nucleos(t)ide analogue (NAs) therapy for the functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in non-cirrhotic adult patients. This approval is based on phase III trial data showing functional cure rates of 19% in adults with HBsAg levels ≤3,000 IU/mL and 26% in those with ≤1,000 IU/mL, compared to less than 1% with standard of care. CHB affects an estimated 75 million people in China, contributing to 450,000 deaths in 2023 and being the leading cause of liver cancer. The approval supports China’s national goal of eliminating hepatitis B as a public health threat by 2030. The trials demonstrated an acceptable safety profile, with common adverse events including injection site erythema, local pain, and temporary liver enzyme elevation. The accelerated approval highlights the potential of bepirovirsen to improve treatment outcomes and address unmet medical needs in CHB patients.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该批准于 2026 年 11 月 26 日宣布。B-Well 三期试验证明了截至第 72 周就诊时的 功能性治愈 率,这很可能在 2026 年进行。
地点与对象
中国,专门针对接受核苷(酸)类似物(NAs)治疗至少6个月且HBV DNA <90 IU/mL且HBsAg水平≤3,000 IU/mL的非肝硬化成人慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB)患者。
事件
中国国家药品监督管理局(NMPA)批准 Hibsago(贝匹罗韦生)联合核苷(酸)类似物(NAs)治疗非肝硬化成人患者的 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎(CHB)。此次批准基于 III 期试验数据,显示与标准护理相比,特定亚组中 功能性治愈 的发生率分别为 19% 和 26%。
规模、影响与风险
Chronic hepatitis B affects an estimated 75 million people in China, contributing to 450,000 deaths in 2023. It is the leading cause of liver cancer in the country, with almost 85% of liver cancer patients having CHB-associated disease. The approval of Hibsago could significantly improve treatment outcomes and support China’s national goal of eliminating hepatitis B as a public health threat by 2030.
应对、状态与不确定性
该批准是根据突破性疗法和优先审评指定授予的,承认中国存在大量未满足的需求。这些试验显示出可接受的安全性和耐受性,最常见的不良事件是注射部位红斑、局部疼痛和肝酶水平暂时升高。该反应基于 III 期试验结果,被认为是 治疗 的重大进步。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

REG - GSK PLC - Hibsago approved in China for chronic hepatitis B - TradingView

News Brief
On 26 November 2026, China’s National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) in combination with nucleos(t)ide analogue (NAs) therapy for the functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in non-cirrhotic adult patients. This approval is based on phase III trial data showing functional cure rates of 19% in adults with HBsAg levels ≤3,000 IU/mL and 26% in those with ≤1,000 IU/mL, compared to less than 1% with standard of care. CHB affects an estimated 75 million people in China, contributing to 450,000 deaths in 2023 and being the leading cause of liver cancer. The approval supports China’s national goal of eliminating hepatitis B as a public health threat by 2030. The trials demonstrated an acceptable safety profile, with common adverse events including injection site erythema, local pain, and temporary liver enzyme elevation. The accelerated approval highlights the potential of bepirovirsen to improve treatment outcomes and address unmet medical needs in CHB patients.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Search Products Community Markets Brokers More EN Black FridayUp to 80% OFF News /London Stock Exchange /REG - GSK PLC - Hibsago approved in China for chronic hepatitis B REG - GSK PLC - Hibsago approved in China for chronic hepatitis B Refinitiv8 min read GSK+1.33% RNS Number : 7207E GSK PLC 26 November 2026 Issued: 26th November 2026, London UK Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in China as a first-in-class functional cure for chronic hepatitis B (CHB) Approval based on phase III data demonstrating unprecedented functional cure rates compared to standard of care[1] CHB affects an estimated 75 million people in China[2] where it is the leading cause of liver cancer[3] China’s Action Plan on Viral Hepatitis[4] commits to at least 80% diagnosis and treatment of new cases by 2030 GSK plc (LSE/NYSE: GSK) today announced that China’s National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) has approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) in combination with nucleos(t)ide analogue (NAs) therapy to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in non-cirrhotic adult patients who are currently on NAs for at least 6 months, and have HBV DNA <90 IU/mL and hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. Bepirovirsen is a first-in-class antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) and offers unprecedented functional cure rates compared to current standard of care alone, with a generally well tolerated safety profile.1 The approval was granted under Breakthrough Therapy and Priority Review designations, recognising the high unmet need in China and potential for substantial clinical improvement over existing therapies.[5],[6] Tony Wood, Chief Scientific Officer, GSK, said: “Chronic hepatitis B affects an estimated 75 million people in China. Despite a strong focus on diagnosis and access to treatment, many patients face a lifetime of therapy and remain at risk of long-term liver complications with current standard of care. The accelerated approval of Hibsago means eligible patients in China now have the potential to achieve functional cure with a six-month treatment course. We’re pleased to bring this important innovation to patients.” The approval is based on pooled results from the B-Well phase III trials demonstrating functional cure rates of 19% in adults with ≤3,000 IU/mL HBsAg, and 26% in those with ≤1,000 IU/mL compared to current standard of care alone1, which typically achieves less than 1% functional cure rate after one year of treatment.[7] Functional cure occurs when the hepatitis B virus DNA and viral protein – HBsAg – are undetectable for at least 24 weeks after stopping all treatment,[8] indicating the disease is controlled by the immune system without medication. Professor Jinlin Hou, Director and Professor, Institute of Hepatology of Guangdong, Nanfang Hospital, Southern Medical University, said: “Chronic hepatitis B remains one of China's most serious public health challenges and the main driver of liver cancer in our country.3 Functional cure is a key treatment goal for eligible patients[9], and this approval gives them a new path towards it after adding a six-month course of bepirovirsen to their nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy. As China expands testing and treatment, this could make a meaningful difference to people living with chronic hepatitis B and support the national goal of eliminating hepatitis B as a public health threat.” CHB is a national health priority in China, where the Action Plan for the Prevention and Control of Viral Hepatitis (2025-2030) sets targets for at least 80% of people living with CHB to be diagnosed, and at least 80% of newly reported patients treated by 2030.4 CHB contributed to an estimated 450,000 deaths in China in 2023[10] and almost 85% of liver cancer patients in the country have CHB-associated disease.3 GSK is collaborating with SBP Group, through its subsidiary Chia Tai Tianqing Pharmaceutical Group Co., Ltd. (CTTQ), to accelerate the launch and access of Hibsago in mainland China. CTTQ is a market leader in hepatology in China with a comprehensive liver disease portfolio and broad commercial footprint with access to more than 5,000 medical centres. Results from the B-Well phase III trials Pooled data from the B-Well phase III trials showed that six-month treatment with bepirovirsen achieved a clinically meaningful 19% functional cure response rate (233 of 1,220 vs. 0 of 614 in the placebo group). B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 each met the primary endpoint, demonstrating a statistically significant improvement in functional cure versus placebo in the overall study population (adults with ≤3,000 IU/mL HBsAg level). In a key secondary endpoint, a functional cure rate of 26% (200 of 768 vs. 0 of 393 in the placebo group) was achieved in participants with ≤1,000 IU/mL HBsAg level,1 a group that represents approximately 45% of diagnosed CHB cases globally.[11] Exploratory analyses also showed bepirovirsen reduced HBsAg to ≤100 IU/mL in 49% of bepirovirsen recipients (598 of 1220) in the overall study population, and in 62% of bepirovirsen recipients in the ≤1,000 IU/mL (476 of 768) subgroup as of the week 72 visit.[12] In medical literature this level of surface antigen (≤100 IU/mL) has been associated with greater immune control of the virus[13], and improved patient outcomes.[14], [15] The trials showed an acceptable safety and tolerability profile consistent with other studies of bepirovirsen. The three most frequently observed adverse events were injection site erythema, local pain and temporary rise in the blood level of a liver enzyme.1 About bepirovirsen Bepirovirsen is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) designed to recognise and inhibit the production of the genetic components (i.e. RNA) of the hepatitis B virus that can lead to chronic disease, potentially allowing a person’s immune system to regain control. Bepirovirsen reduces the production of RNA and viral proteins associated with HBV, suppresses the level of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood,1 and may stimulate the immune system to increase the chances of a durable and sustained response. GSK licensed bepirovirsen from Ionis Pharmaceuticals and collaborated with them on its development. Hibsago is approved in China, Japan and the US. It has been recognised by global regulatory authorities for its innovation and potential to address significant unmet need in hepatitis B, with Priority Review and Breakthrough Therapy designations from the US FDA, Breakthrough Therapy and Priority Review designations in China and SENKU designation in Japan. About the B-Well trials The B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials are global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials conducted in 29 countries. They assessed the efficacy, safety, pharmacokinetic profile, and durability of functional cure in nucleos(t)ide analogue-treated adult participants with chronic hepatitis B and baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3,000 IU/mL. The primary endpoint assessed the proportion of participants achieving functional cure in patients with baseline HBsAg ≤3,000 IU/mL (assessed six months after all therapies were discontinued). A key ranked secondary endpoint evaluated functional cure in participants with baseline HBsAg ≤1,000 IU/mL. About chronic hepatitis B Hepatitis B is a viral infection that can cause both acute and chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B occurs when the immune system is unable to clear the virus, resulting in long-lasting infection that affects more than 240 million people worldwide.10 The disease causes approximately 1.1 million deaths each year and is the leading cause of liver cancer cases globally[16]. Currently many patients often require lifelong antiviral therapy for viral suppression, making functional cure a critical goal in disease management. Achieving functional cure, marked by the loss of HBsAg and undetectable HBV DNA after stopping treatment, is associated with a reduction in the risk of long-term liver complications, including liver cancer.[17] About GSK’s hepatology portfolio GSK is extending its expertise in inflammation to develop a next wave of innovation for the millions of people affected by chronic and life-threatening fibro-inflammatory liver conditions. GSK has a growing hepatology pipeline, harnessed by the science of the immune system and advanced technologies, with a focus on chronic hepatitis B, metabolic dysfunction-associated steatohepatitis (MASH) and alcohol-associated liver disease (ALD). About GSK GSK is a global biopharma company with a purpose to unite science, technology, and talent to get ahead of disease together. Find out more at www.gsk.com. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

公共卫生警报报告显示,美国新生儿乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 发病率下降

Hepatitis B vaccination rates in US newborns falling, Public Health Alerts report shows
新闻简报A new report from Public Health Alerts highlights a concerning decline in hepatitis B vaccination rates among US newborns. The rate dropped from 80.0% in 2020-2022 to 65.6% in June 2026, following a federal policy change that shifted from universal vaccination to individual decision-making for infants of mothers testing negative for HBV. The report estimates that over 640 infants annually are born to mothers with HBV infection who are not tested, and about 360,000 are born to mothers who aren’t screened for the virus. This poses a significant risk of perinatal HBV infection, which can lead to chronic hepatitis B. The study, conducted by Truveta and the Callen-Lorde Community Health Center, analyzed data from 2018 to June 2026, covering 1.3 million infants. The authors stress the importance of the hepatitis B birth dose as a critical safety measure against gaps in protection. The report also notes that the revised childhood immunization schedule, which reduced recommended vaccines, was blocked by a federal judge in March 2026. Public Health Alerts, a collaboration between NEJM Evidence and CIDRAP, aims to provide reliable data and expert insights for public health decision-making.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发布于2026年11月24日。分析的数据涵盖2018年至2026年6月。疫苗接种费率的下降始于2023年中期,并于2026年6月降至65.6%。
地点与对象
The United States, focusing on newborns and pregnant women. The study analyzed data on 1.3 million infants born to 1.1 million women across the country.
事件
美国新生儿中的乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 率已从 2020 年至 2022 年的 80.0% 下降到 2026 年 6 月的 65.6%。报告强调,每年有超过 640 名婴儿是由未接受乙肝病毒感染的母亲所生,约 360,000 名婴儿是由未接受病毒筛查的母亲所生。这种下降归因于联邦 疫苗 指南的变化,该指南从普遍建议转变为针对乙肝病毒检测呈阴性的母亲的婴儿的个人决策。
规模、影响与风险
该报告估计,每年有 360,000 名孕妇没有接受乙肝病毒筛查,超过 640 名婴儿是由未接受乙肝病毒感染的母亲所生。这些婴儿面临围产期 HBV 感染的风险,可能导致 慢性乙型肝炎。2026 年 6 月,新生儿 疫苗接种 率下降至 65.6%,低于 2022 年的 79.8%。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生警报是 NEJM Evidence 和 CIDRAP 之间的合作项目,提供经过专家评审的报告,将一线观察结果转化为可操作的公共卫生证据。该报告指出,修订后的儿童免疫接种计划减少了推荐的 疫苗 数量,但该计划于 2026 年 3 月被联邦法官阻止。作者强调了乙型肝炎出生剂量作为应对保护缺口的关键安全网的重要性。
